Experiment journal -- cosmos_distill_experiments (KD static distillation sweep)

============================================================
2026-08-17 -- node throughput profiling, sweep progress check, resume + eval launch
============================================================

--- Node throughput profiling (jobs 19118341 / 19118342) ---

Ran cosmos_policy.scripts.cosmos_distill_experiments.kd.profile_node_throughput on rg31702 and
rg21706 (one job per node, pinned via --nodelist), to check whether the two nodes used by the
KD sweep have comparable throughput. The script reproduces train_kd_static.py's exact
per-iteration compute (real+synthetic denoise() forwards, combined_kd_loss, backward,
optimizer.step()) against the real kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn dataset dirs, with a
random-init student (skip_load_model=True -- measuring hardware/compute cost, not model
correctness) at batch_size=8, 5 warmup + 30 timed iterations.

Result: the two nodes are essentially identical.
  phase (avg/iter)     rg31702    rg21706
  data                  0.0067s    0.0067s
  real_forward          0.0939s    0.0938s
  synthetic_forward     0.0932s    0.0928s
  backward              0.5188s    0.5171s
  opt_step              0.0094s    0.0094s
  total                 0.7219s    0.7197s
  implied iter/sec      1.385      1.389
  implied time/30k iter 6.02 hr    6.00 hr

Both hit 100% GPU utilization at steady state, ~353W/700W power draw, P0, no throttle reasons
active, temps 54-57C. Backward pass dominates iteration time (~72%); the two forward passes are
~13% each; data loading is negligible (<1%). Conclusion: no node-to-node variance worth
correcting for -- whichever of these two nodes a run lands on, per-iteration throughput should be
the same. GPUs are compute-bound (100% util) but not power-bound (~50% of cap), consistent with
kernels not fully saturating the SMs rather than being data-starved or thermally throttled.

--- 3rd-launch sweep progress check (jobs 19070841-19070846) ---

Checked how far the 3rd launch of the 6-variant KD sweep (3 agent variants: action-only /
action+value / action+value+future-state, x 2 data variants: teacher-analogous synthetic only /
+ perturbed-action synthetic -- see kd_static_syn_tn_launch_2026-08-15.txt for full launch
history) got before its jobs ended. All 6 started 2026-08-15T10:50:49 with a 12hr walltime and a
30000-iteration target (checkpoint_every=100). Checked final iteration by reading the highest
iter_NNNNNNNNN checkpoint dir under each run's checkpoints/.

  job_id    run (variant)                      node      final iter   status
  19070841  syn_tn      (action+value+future)   rg31702   27,600       TIMEOUT (12hr limit)
  19070842  syn_tn_pa   (action+value+future)   rg31702   27,700       TIMEOUT (12hr limit)
  19070843  av_syn_tn   (action+value)          rg31702   27,600       TIMEOUT (12hr limit)
  19070844  av_syn_tn_pa (action+value)         rg21706   29,999       COMPLETED
  19070845  action_syn_tn (action only)         rg21706   29,999       COMPLETED
  19070846  action_syn_tn_pa (action only)      rg21706   29,999       COMPLETED

The 3 rg21706 jobs (all lighter loss terms -- action-only or action+value, no future-state
prediction) finished cleanly. The 3 rg31702 jobs (action+value+future-state, or action+value with
the heavier _pa merged dataset) ran the full 12hr walltime and stopped ~2,300-2,400 iterations
short. Given the node-throughput profiling above showed no real hardware difference between
rg31702 and rg21706, the shortfall is attributed to these being the heavier-loss variants, not a
node speed issue.

--- 4th launch: resume the 3 timed-out runs + launch eval jobs for all 6 variants ---

Resumed the 3 TIMEOUT'd training runs from their last checkpoints (auto-resume via

for all 6 syn_tn/syn_tn_pa variants, which had none before (only the older non-synthetic-data
baseline variants -- kd_static_500m_std_0_05[_action|_av]_eval.yaml -- had eval configs).

periodic_libero_eval_static.py pattern -- run_type: kd_static_eval, watches a
monitored_job_name's checkpoints/ as they land, libero_object suite, 3 trials/task = 30
episodes/checkpoint, restart-safe via eval_results.csv, 40G mem, 23:59:00 walltime):
  conf/runs/kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn_eval.yaml
  conf/runs/kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn_pa_eval.yaml

============================================================
2026-08-17 -- eval job status check, syn_tn eval crash diagnosed + fixed
============================================================

--- Status check on the 4th-launch jobs above ---

19143217/18/19 (resume-train) all COMPLETED cleanly in ~35min each; all 6 kd_static_500m_std_0_05
[_action|_av]_syn_tn[_pa] variants confirmed at checkpoints/latest_checkpoint.txt = iter_000029999
(the 30000-iter target). Of the 6 continuous eval companions (19143220-19143225): 5 are healthy
(RUNNING, all `ok` rows in eval_results.csv, ~15min/checkpoint, at checkpoint ~3300/30000 after
~9hr -- 0.0% success rate so far at these early checkpoints, not itself concerning this early).

--- syn_tn eval (19143220) diagnosed as broken, node-specific -- fixed via node exclusion ---

19143220 (kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn_eval) had 251/251 checkpoint evals fail with exit -6
(SIGABRT) since it started -- 100% failure rate, zero valid results ever produced. No Python
traceback in eval_failure_iter_*.log; each crash is silent, mid-rollout. Root-caused by:
  - Crash point is bit-identical across every checkpoint including iter_000000000 (init weights,
    no real training yet) -- same task ("pick up the alphabet soup and place it in the basket",
    LIBERO-object task index 3), same episode (3rd of 3, seed=7/randomize_seed=False so fully
    deterministic), same sim timestep (t=234) every single time. Not model/data dependent.
  - 19143220 was the only one of the 6 eval jobs scheduled on node rg31609 (others landed on
    rg31602 x3, rg13502, rg13602) -- pointing at a node-specific issue rather than a code bug.
  - ssh'd to rg31609 (pam_slurm_adopt put the session in 19143220's own cgroup): nvidia-smi showed
    the GPU healthy (97% util, 7.4/81GB mem, P0, no ECC pending-retirement flags) while the job was
    running, so no smoking gun there; couldn't read dmesg (permission denied) to check for Xid
    errors, so the exact node-level mechanism (driver/EGL quirk vs transient GPU fault vs
    contention with the 3 other concurrently-running unifesp-peft jobs also on rg31609) is
    unconfirmed. Given the crash reproduces on iter 0 weights and only on this one node, node-level
    is the best-supported explanation available.

Fix: scancel'd 19143220, then resubmitted the existing kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn_eval.sbatch
directly via `sbatch --exclude=rg31609 <script>` (submit_sweep.py/RunConfig has no built-in
node-exclude field, so this bypassed it) -- new job 19153067, currently PENDING (Priority, not a
resource block) waiting for scheduling. Starts over from iter 0 (nothing to resume -- 0 checkpoints
had ever succeeded). If it also fails on whatever node it lands on, that would point back toward a
code/data issue specific to the syn_tn variant rather than rg31609 specifically, and would need the
eval_failure_iter_*.log conclusion above revisited.

Next: once 19153067 starts running, check its eval_results.csv for `ok` rows (i.e. that it clears
past iter 0 without repeating the SIGABRT) before trusting it's fixed. Also revisit the 5 healthy
eval jobs once they reach higher iterations to see whether success rate climbs off 0%.

============================================================
2026-08-17 (later same day) -- CORRECTION: node-exclude fix above was a red herring. Real cause:
$HOME inode quota exhaustion. All 6 eval jobs killed, video-write path patched, all 6 relaunched.
============================================================

--- The node-rg31609 diagnosis above was wrong ---

19153067 (syn_tn eval, resubmitted with --exclude=rg31609) landed on rg31503 and FAILED AGAIN,
this time much faster (~10-40s/checkpoint instead of ~15min) and with a full Python traceback this
time (earlier failure logs had none -- turned out to just be bad luck about where `tail` landed,
not a different failure mode). The traceback: `OSError: [Errno 32] Broken pipe` out of
imageio_ffmpeg, caused by `Error opening output ... Disk quota exceeded` when
save_rollout_video() tried to write an .mp4. So the crash was never node-specific -- rg31609 was a
coincidence of scheduling, not the cause. Checked `diskusage_report --home`: 499K/500K files (44/50GB
space -- it's the *file-count* quota, not space, that was exhausted). This is a $HOME-wide,
cluster-wide limit, independent of which compute node a job lands on, which is exactly why
excluding rg31609 didn't help and why the "same task/episode/timestep every time" determinism
(true, and still the right observation) had the wrong explanation attached to it.

--- All 6 eval jobs were affected, not just syn_tn, and were making it worse ---

Checked the other 5 (previously "healthy") eval jobs' eval_results.csv: all 5 had ALSO started
appending `failed` rows starting ~09:43, the same time the quota tipped over, now failing every
~10s instead of every ~15min -- i.e. racing through all remaining checkpoints and writing a new
eval_failure_iter_*.log per checkpoint, each attempted .mp4 write consuming more of an already-
exhausted inode quota. This was a runaway-worse-not-better loop. Killed all 6
(19143221/22/23/24/25 + 19153067) via scancel.

--- Root cause of the inode exhaustion, and the actual fix ---

save_rollout_video() / save_rollout_video_with_future_image_predictions() in
cosmos_policy/experiments/robot/libero/libero_utils.py wrote 2 .mp4s per episode (regular +
with-future-predictions) to a hardcoded `./rollouts/{DATE}` -- relative to cwd, which every
kd_static_eval sbatch script cd's to
/home/esraa1/code/efficient-wm-at-scale/cosmos_policy before running, so every single rollout
video from every continuous eval job, across the whole KD sweep's history, was landing under
$HOME instead of the /project storage the checkpoints/eval_results.csv/failure logs already use.
No existing flag to disable video-saving. ~30 episodes/checkpoint x 2 videos x up to 300
checkpoints x 6 variants was always going to hit $HOME's 500K-file ceiling eventually; today was
just the day it did.

Fix (code change, not a workaround): added a `rollout_dir` parameter to both functions in
libero_utils.py (defaults to the old `./rollouts/{DATE}` behavior if not passed, so nothing else
that imports these -- run_libero_eval.py is the only caller -- breaks), and updated
run_libero_eval.py's two call sites to pass `cfg.local_log_dir`, mirroring the existing
`rollout_data_dir = os.path.join(cfg.local_log_dir, "rollout_data")` pattern a few lines below.
Since periodic_libero_eval_static.py already passes `--local_log_dir` as the eval job's own
project-storage run_dir, this moves all future rollout video writes off $HOME entirely with no
sbatch/config changes needed.

Also freed some immediate headroom: cleared cosmos_policy/.uv_tmp (activate_cuda_env.sh sets this
as $TMPDIR -- ~13.8K stale wandb/uv staging files dating back to 2026-07-28, confirmed nothing had
any of it open via lsof once all jobs were killed). Went from 499K/500K to 493K, then it settled
further to 475K/500K on its own a few minutes later (likely killed-job atexit temp cleanup
finishing). Did NOT touch the ~15.8K existing rollout videos under
cosmos_policy/rollouts/{2026_07_28..2026_08_17} -- those are past-experiment eval artifacts, not
scratch, left for the user to decide on separately.

Also stripped the bogus `failed` rows that all 6 jobs wrote during the quota-exhaustion episode
from each variant's eval_results.csv (keeping real `ok` rows; timestamped `.bak_*` copies of every
CSV made before editing) -- otherwise the same load_already_evaluated() "any status counts as
done" behavior noted on 2026-08-17 (first entry) would have permanently skipped those iterations
too.

--- Relaunch ---

Resubmitted all 6 via `sbatch <variant>_eval.sbatch` (no --exclude needed anymore -- node was never
the issue):
  job_id     run (variant)
  19155582   kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn_eval
  19155583   kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn_pa_eval
  19155584   kd_static_500m_std_0_05_av_syn_tn_eval
  19155585   kd_static_500m_std_0_05_av_syn_tn_pa_eval
  19155586   kd_static_500m_std_0_05_action_syn_tn_eval
  19155587   kd_static_500m_std_0_05_action_syn_tn_pa_eval
All 6 PENDING (Reason=Priority, not a resource/config problem) as of this writing; 19155582's
StartTime estimate ~12:48 (~2.5hr queue wait, ordinary cluster contention).

Next: once these start running, confirm (a) eval_results.csv rows come back `ok` again, (b)
`diskusage_report --home` file count stays flat rather than climbing back toward 500K (proof
videos are actually landing under the project-storage local_log_dir now, not $HOME) -- check a
run's local_log_dir/rollouts/{DATE}/ directly if unsure. If home's count starts climbing again,
the fix didn't fully take (e.g. some other write path still hits $HOME) and needs re-diagnosis
rather than another node-exclude-style guess.

============================================================
2026-08-17 (follow-up) -- video fix confirmed; rg31609 re-crashed a 2nd variant, node-exclude
re-applied (this time on stronger evidence, not a guess)
============================================================

--- Video-path fix confirmed ---

All 6 jobs from the relaunch above (19155582-19155587) started running by ~10:20-10:33. Checked
`diskusage_report --home`: 476K/500K files, flat (was 475K right after the 2026-08-17 cleanup) --
not climbing back toward the 500K ceiling despite hours of eval activity across all 6 variants.
Confirmed each *_eval run's rollouts/ directory now exists directly under its project-storage
local_log_dir (e.g. .../cosmos_distill_experiments_kd_static_500m_std_0_05_action_syn_tn_eval/
rollouts/), not under cosmos_policy/rollouts (i.e. not $HOME-relative) -- the libero_utils.py
rollout_dir fix is working as intended.

--- action_syn_tn eval (19155586) landed on rg31609 and reproduced the exact same silent-crash
signature as the earlier syn_tn/rg31609 episode ---

5 of 6 jobs (syn_tn, syn_tn_pa, av_syn_tn, av_syn_tn_pa, action_syn_tn_pa) are healthy, appending
`ok` rows. 19155586 (action_syn_tn_eval) landed on rg31609 -- the same node implicated (then
dismissed as a red herring in favor of the $HOME quota explanation) in the earlier 2026-08-17
entry -- and started failing every checkpoint immediately: 3600-4100 all `failed`, no traceback in
any eval_failure_iter_*.log (checked 4100's in full, 1460 lines, no Error/Exception/Traceback/
Abort string anywhere), crash consistently occurring mid-rollout around t=234 in the log's last
INFO line before it cuts off -- the same "silent, mid-rollout, fixed sim timestep" signature as
the original syn_tn crashes. This time the $HOME quota was independently ruled out first
(diskusage_report showed 476K/500K, flat, and this run's rollout videos confirmed landing under
its project-storage local_log_dir, not $HOME) and `sacct` showed the parent Slurm job itself
running cleanly (ExitCode 0:0, State RUNNING) -- only the per-checkpoint eval subprocess is
crashing, not the job. Checked rg31609's GPU again via ssh (pam_slurm_adopt into the job's cgroup):
no ECC errors, no retired/remapped pages, 54% util, P0, healthy by every counter nvidia-smi
exposes -- still no permission to read dmesg for Xid errors, so the exact mechanism (driver quirk,
EGL/rendering library issue specific to this node, GPU fault below ECC's detection threshold) is
still unconfirmed. But this is now two independent jobs, on two different days' worth of training
checkpoints, both crashing 100%-reproducibly on this one node with an identical signature and nowhere
else -- strong enough to treat as a real rg31609-specific issue rather than the coincidence it was
first assumed to be.

Fix: confirmed with the user before cancelling a running job (scancel is a shared-cluster-state
action, not auto-approved). scancel'd 19155586, resubmitted via
`sbatch --exclude=rg31609 kd_static_500m_std_0_05_action_syn_tn_eval.sbatch` -- new job 19156930,
PENDING (Priority). Restart-safe (eval_results.csv already has `ok` through iter 3500), so nothing
is lost by the restart; it resumes from iter 3600.

Next: once 19156930 starts, confirm it clears past iter 3600 with `ok` rows on whatever
(non-rg31609) node it lands on. If rg31609 keeps producing this same failure for other jobs in the
future, it's worth flagging to cluster admins directly (mechanism still unconfirmed without dmesg
access) rather than re-diagnosing from scratch each time -- consider excluding rg31609 by default
in future kd_static_eval submissions until it's cleared.

--- 19156930 confirmed running clean, no new rg31609-style crash ---

19156930 started 2026-08-17T10:45:10 on rg21804 (not rg31609). As of ~10:52 (7min in, no new
eval_results.csv row yet) it had produced zero new `failed` rows -- a good sign on its own, since
the rg31609 crash signature was near-instant (~2min/checkpoint, failing every single one). The
other 5 jobs (19155582-19155585, 19155587) remained healthy throughout, and `diskusage_report
--home` stayed flat at 476K/500K. Started a background poll (Monitor tool) on
action_syn_tn_eval's eval_results.csv to catch the first fresh row once it lands, rather than
manually re-polling.

19156930 produced its first fresh row shortly after: iter 4600, `ok`, at 2026-08-17T11:01:00 --
confirms the rg31609 exclusion fixed it (a crash would have shown up as `failed` within ~2min of
job start, as it did every time on rg31609; instead this took the normal ~15min-per-checkpoint
eval time and succeeded). All 6 kd_static_500m_std_0_05[_action|_av]_syn_tn[_pa]_eval jobs are now
healthy and progressing. Nothing further to do here unless rg31609 causes trouble again elsewhere.

--- Clarified: eval trial count is per-task, not per-checkpoint-total ---

User asked whether the KD sweep eval configs (`eval_num_trials_per_task: 3`) run 30 episodes per
*task*. Confirmed via a full-suite libero_object eval log
(logs/ENV_EVAL-libero_object-cosmos-2026_08_04-20_28_01--...--full_suite.txt, "Number of tasks: 10")
that libero_object has 10 tasks -- so eval_num_trials_per_task: 3 means 3 trials/task, and the
"30 episodes/checkpoint" figure already used elsewhere in this journal (e.g. the 2026-08-17 first
entry's kd_static_eval description) is 10 tasks x 3 trials, not 30 trials on a single task. No
config change, just a documentation clarification in case "30" gets referenced again without the
per-task/per-checkpoint distinction being obvious from context.

2026-08-17 (later) -- eval progress check, success-rate-vs-checkpoint chart,
flat-0%-success investigation, and a training extension to 60,000 iterations
============================================================

--- Job status re-check: all 6 eval companions RUNNING and healthy ---

Re-checked squeue + each variant's eval_results.csv. All 6 continuous eval jobs
(19155582/83/84/85/87, 19156930) RUNNING, none on rg31609, all `ok` rows at their latest
checkpoint. `diskusage_report --home` still flat at 476K/500K -- video-path fix from earlier
today continues to hold. action_syn_tn_eval's 10 `failed` rows (iters 3600-4500, timestamped
10:21-10:41) are the leftover record of the original rg31609 crash episode from before its
19156930 resubmit with --exclude=rg31609 -- expected, not new.

Checkpoint progress at this check: syn_tn 4700, syn_tn_pa 8300, av_syn_tn 8200, av_syn_tn_pa
8100, action_syn_tn 9100-9200, action_syn_tn_pa 8100 -- all still well short of the (then)
30000-iter target, so no variant's full 0-30000 range had been evaluated yet.

--- Published a success-rate-vs-checkpoint chart (Artifact) ---

Built a line chart (6 series, one per variant, hover crosshair/tooltip + table-view toggle) of
`success_rate` vs `iteration` from each variant's eval_results.csv, published as an Artifact
("KD Sweep Eval Progress"). Snapshot, not live -- would need re-publishing against fresh CSVs to
stay current. At publish time every series was flat at 0% except one point: action_syn_tn hit
3.3% (1/30) at iter 8600.

--- Investigated the flat-0%-success pattern -- concluded likely normal, not a bug ---

Checked train_loss.csv for these runs: loss drops smoothly and monotonically from ~0.4-0.65 at
init to ~0.01-0.08 by iter 8500, continuing down to ~0.005 by iter 29990 (a further >10x drop
past where eval currently sits) -- training itself is healthy, no divergence/plateau/NaNs.

Verified the eval harness itself can register real success: a 2026-07-29 reference log
(logs/ENV_EVAL-libero_object-cosmos-2026_07_29-07_55_17_results.csv) shows 90-100% success across
all 10 libero_object tasks, so the pipeline isn't silently broken.

Found a directly comparable historical precedent in logs/*_results.csv from 2026-08-04: earlier
"tiny" single/multi-task models trained with the plain joint objective stayed at 0% success
through iterations 2000-9900, but `cosmos_dit_wm_tiny_object_suite_action_only` (action-only
objective) first showed real success (up to 60% on some tasks) around iteration 7400 -- closely
matching the current sweep's only nonzero point: action_syn_tn (this sweep's action-only variant)
at iter 8600.

Conclusion: the other 5 variants (action+value, or action+value+future-state) split gradient
signal across auxiliary losses instead of dedicating it entirely to the action head, on a much
bigger 500M-parameter student and a more diverse 10-task dataset than the 2026-08-04 tiny-model
precedent -- a harder optimization problem, so needing more iterations before crossing into
nonzero-success territory is expected. Also, `eval_num_trials_per_task: 3` (30 episodes/checkpoint
total) is a small enough sample that a genuinely-low-but-nonzero true success rate can easily roll
zero successes by chance for a while -- some of the flatness may be detection resolution, not
true 0% competence. No code/config changes made from this investigation; noted as a documentation
finding only.

--- Extended all 6 variants' training target from 30,000 to 60,000 iterations ---

User asked to continue training all 6 variants for another 30k iterations. All 6 had already
COMPLETED cleanly at iter_000029999 (per the first 2026-08-17 entry) and each had written a
`TRAINING_DONE` marker in its run_dir.

Two things needed to happen together, not just a resubmit:
  - `max_iter: 30000` -> `60000` edited in all 6 conf/runs/kd_static_500m_std_0_05_*.yaml files
    (train_kd_static[_av|_action].py's loop is `for iteration in itertools.count(): ... if
    iteration >= params.max_iter: break`, and resume sets `start_iteration = last_iteration + 1`
    from the latest checkpoint, so raising max_iter and resuming continues cleanly from 30000).
  - Deleted the stale `TRAINING_DONE` marker in each of the 6 run_dirs BEFORE resubmitting.
    Reason: periodic_libero_eval_static.py's stop condition is `training_done_path.exists() and no
    unevaluated checkpoints remain` (re-checked live each 30s poll, not cached at eval-job
    startup) -- with the stale marker still present, once each eval companion caught up to
    checkpoint 29999 it would conclude training was finished and exit, even though 30000 more
    checkpoints were about to start landing. Clearing the marker first means the eval jobs (still
    only at checkpoints ~4700-9200 as of this check, so no race) will just keep polling and pick
    up the new 30000-59999 checkpoints as they land, with no separate action needed on the eval
    side.

Resubmitted via submit_sweep.py (piped `yes` past the interactive "already has a checkpoint...
resume?" confirmation prompt for all 6, same prompt noted in the 4th-launch entry above):
  .venv/bin/python -m cosmos_policy.scripts.cosmos_distill_experiments.submit_sweep \
    launch.only=[kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn,kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn_pa,\

--- Resume confirmed for all 6 (the background log-grep check was a false alarm) ---

The background poll for "Resuming from ... at iteration 30000" in each job's slurm .out log timed
out after 5min with no match on any of the 6 -- looked concerning, but turned out to be a
buffering artifact, not a stall: train_kd_static[_av|_action].py's `print()` calls go to stdout,
which is block-buffered when redirected to a file (not a TTY), while the CRITICAL/WARNING lines
from Python's logging module (student-checkpoint load messages) go to stderr and flush
immediately -- so the slurm log's last visible line was stuck at the same
`_IncompatibleKeys(...)` load_state_dict message for all 6, well past when resume must have
actually happened. Checked train_loss.csv directly instead (written via csv.writer, not subject to
the same stdout buffering) and confirmed all 6 were already well past iteration 30000:

  variant             last logged iter   latest_checkpoint.txt
  syn_tn              30300              iter_000030300
  syn_tn_pa           30250              iter_000030200
  av_syn_tn           30300              iter_000030300
  av_syn_tn_pa        30250              iter_000030200
  action_syn_tn       30300              iter_000030200
  action_syn_tn_pa    30240              iter_000030200

All 6 resumed cleanly from iter_000029999 and are producing new checkpoints past the old 30000
target, confirming both the max_iter bump and the TRAINING_DONE-marker fix worked as intended.
Lesson for next time: don't trust a quiet slurm .out log alone as a stall signal for these
training scripts -- check train_loss.csv/checkpoints/latest_checkpoint.txt first, they're not
subject to the print()-buffering delay.

Next: once these 6 jobs progress further, re-check eval_results.csv for checkpoints past 30000 to
confirm the eval companions are picking them up with no gap (expected, given they poll
checkpoint_dir directly every 30s rather than relying on the training job's stdout). Re-publish
the success-rate chart once more checkpoints -- especially the still-mostly-untested 9000-30000+
range -- have been evaluated, to see whether the other 5 variants cross into nonzero success the
way action_syn_tn did.

2026-08-18 -- loss-curve check, rollout video/frame comparison across all 6 variants,

--- Loss curves published (Artifact: "KD Sweep Loss Curves") ---

Pulled train_loss.csv for all 6 variants (binned/smoothed over 200-iteration windows) and
published a two-panel log-scale chart: total loss (NOT comparable across objective families --
syn_tn/syn_tn_pa sum 8 loss slots, av_* sum 2, action_* sums 1, so a lower total loss for the
action-only variants partly reflects fewer summed terms) and action-prediction loss alone (IS
comparable, since every variant's objective includes an action-chunk term). All 6 decreasing
smoothly through the 30000-iter extension boundary, no divergence/NaNs. action_syn_tn has the
lowest action loss at the current tail (~0.0038-0.0039), consistent with it being the only variant
with a nonzero eval-success point so far. av_syn_tn_pa is the noisiest/highest of the 6 (spikes up
to ~0.11 on individual batches) -- real per-batch variance in the heaviest merged-dataset ("_pa")
variant, not a bug.

--- Rollout video/frame comparison across all 6 variants, 4 tasks (Artifacts) ---

Confirmed video recording is on for every eval job: run_libero_eval.py unconditionally calls
save_rollout_video() + save_rollout_video_with_future_image_predictions() every episode (2 mp4s
each, no flag to disable), landing under each *_eval run's project-storage
local_log_dir/rollouts/{DATE}/ since the earlier libero_utils.py fix. All 6 variants confirmed
actively writing new videos (~3100-3200 mp4s each as of the check).

Compared episode-1 rollouts for 4 tasks (alphabet_soup, butter, ketchup, bbq_sauce) across all 6
variants at their then-current checkpoints (~iter 5300-9700) -- first as a static 6x4 frame-grid
montage (frames at ~8/36/64/92% of each 9.3s episode, built via ffmpeg+PIL), then, on request, as
an actual playable video-grid version (24 embedded mp4 clips, autoplay/muted/loop + a pause-all
toggle, respects prefers-reduced-motion). Both published as Artifacts ("KD Sweep Rollout
Comparison" and "KD Sweep Rollout Videos").

Findings (eval uses a fixed seed -- seed=7, randomize_seed=False -- so every variant starts from a
bit-identical scene per task; differences are purely learned behavior):
  - None of the 6 discriminate by the language-specified target -- every arm reaches toward
    whichever object is nearest/leftmost in the scene, not necessarily the one the instruction
    names (clearest in "butter" and "bbq_sauce"). Reads as generic "reach toward what's closest"
    rather than task-conditioned targeting -- expected this early, worth re-checking later.
  - None of the 24 episodes shown get an object to the basket -- consistent with the ~0% success
    rate across the board; whatever's emerging so far looks like early reach/contact behavior, not
    the transport-and-place step LIBERO success actually requires.
  - No single variant is a consistent behavioral stand-out: av_syn_tn_pa looked most decisive in
    the first two tasks checked (a clean grasp-and-lift in "butter" no other variant achieved
    there), but that lead didn't hold over ketchup/bbq_sauce, where action_syn_tn_pa and syn_tn
    showed comparable or more visible object displacement instead. Read as per-episode noise on
    very early checkpoints, not a settled ranking -- corrected from an initial 2-task read that
    over-indexed on av_syn_tn_pa before the 3rd/4th task complicated it.
  - action_syn_tn (lowest action loss, the only nonzero-success variant so far) showed no visibly
    different or more competent rollout in any of these 4 episodes -- its earlier 3.3% success
    point looks like a rare, checkpoint-specific event, not yet a visible behavioral edge.

--- Clarified: training data spans all 4 LIBERO suites; eval only covers libero_object ---

User asked whether training uses data across all suites. Confirmed via
conf/runs/datasets/build_distill_dataset_std_0.05.yaml (the real-data term all 6 variants share)
and build_teacher_native_distill_dataset.yaml / build_synthetic_distill_dataset.yaml (the syn_tn /
_pa synthetic terms) -- all three built with
`suites: [libero_object_regen, libero_goal_regen, libero_spatial_regen, libero_10_regen]` plus real
rollout trajectories (all_episodes). So yes, all 4 suites, not just libero_object.

Noted as a real asymmetry worth keeping in mind for the flat-success investigation above: these
students are being trained on a broader, more diffuse distribution (4 suites' worth of objects/
scenes/goals) than what continuous eval measures (libero_object only, 10 tasks) -- so their
convergence speed on the libero_object eval metric isn't directly comparable to a
libero_object-only-trained reference model (e.g. the 2026-08-04 "tiny" precedent cited in the
flat-success investigation), which had an easier, narrower target for the same metric.

list and the TRAINING_DONE-stop check) pass `args.eval_every_n_iters` through. Added

============================================================
2026-08-18 (later still) -- loss-spike/optimizer-state check (ruled out), perturbed-action
mechanics clarified, and a real explanation for why action_syn_tn_pa trails action_syn_tn
============================================================

--- Hypothesis checked and ruled out: loss spikes are NOT from a lost optimizer state on resume ---

User asked whether the per-batch loss spikes (av_syn_tn_pa in particular, up to ~0.11-0.34 on
individual batches -- see the 2026-08-18 loss-curve entry) could be caused by the optimizer
re-initializing (fresh Adam moments) on every walltime-timeout resume, rather than restoring saved
state. Checked both the code and the data:
  - Code: checkpoint_io.py's save_versioned_checkpoint writes train_state.pt as
    {"model": ..., "optimizer": optimizer.state_dict(), "iteration": ...} -- full Adam moments
    included. All three training scripts (train_kd_static.py, train_kd_static_av.py,
    train_kd_static_action.py) construct a fresh AdamW on startup but immediately call
    optimizer.load_state_dict(optimizer_state) when resume_state is not None, before the training
    loop runs -- so a resume never actually trains with reinitialized moments.
  - Data: checked the two known resume boundaries directly. av_syn_tn's walltime resume at iter
    27600->27610: loss stays in the normal 0.004-0.03 range right after resume; nearest spike
    (0.137) is 50 iterations later, and another (0.090) happened *before* the resume at iter 27550.
    av_syn_tn_pa's max_iter-bump resume at iter 29990->30000: loss at 30000 (0.0129) is actually
    *lower* than at 29990 (0.0210) -- no discontinuity at all. Spikes across av_syn_tn_pa's full
    history are scattered at essentially random iterations (780, 14040, 35060, 190, 15170, ...)
    with no clustering near either resume boundary.
Conclusion: ruled out. Spikes look like ordinary per-batch gradient variance in the heaviest
merged-dataset variant, not a resume artifact.

--- Clarified what "perturbed-action" (_pa) synthetic data actually perturbs ---

User asked whether the _pa data's action TARGETS are perturbed (i.e. is the student trained to
reproduce a noised action value). Traced through kd/synthetic_generation.py and
build_synthetic_distill_dataset.py: no -- the perturbation is applied to the ACTION FED AS INPUT
CONDITIONING to the teacher's rollout, not to any label the student is trained against.
perturb_action (synthetic_generation.py:121-126) adds clipped Gaussian noise to the recorded
action; generate_synthetic_targets then holds that perturbed action FIXED as conditioning while
sampling the teacher's own future_proprio/future_wrist_image/future_image/value for that
off-trajectory candidate (module docstring calls this the "counterfactual" branch, as opposed to
"teacher-native" where the action itself is also freely generated). splice_synthetic_targets_into_
batch forces world_model_sample_mask=1/value_function_sample_mask=0 specifically because "the
perturbed action is a GIVEN input here, not something to ask the model to 'predict'" -- confirmed
at training time too: batch_prep.py's split_synthetic_action_distill_loss shows the action-slot
denoising target is clamped to ground-truth regardless of network output for these examples, so
the distill loss on that half is always exactly 0 by construction.

Read (with the caveat that this is an inferred rationale, not a quoted code comment) as a
DAgger-style counterfactual-augmentation mechanism: real demo/rollout data only ever shows the
future-state/value heads the one action the teacher actually took at each state, so perturbing the
input action and asking the teacher for the resulting future state/value widens the (state,
action) coverage those two heads are trained on, without needing new real rollouts -- addressing
the covariate-shift problem where the student's own imperfect actions drift off the demonstrated
trajectory during rollout.

--- Root-caused why action_syn_tn_pa trails action_syn_tn on action loss (from the earlier
"are perturbed actions helping at all" comparison, where average action_loss over training was
+46% to +73% worse for _pa variants and widening over time) ---

Given the mechanism above -- perturbed-action examples contribute ~0 gradient to any loss term
except future-state/value -- checked what train_kd_static_action.py (the action-only family) is
actually being trained on for the _pa variant. Confirmed via the two run configs:
  - kd_static_500m_std_0_05_action_syn_tn.yaml: synthetic_dataset_dir =
    kd_teacher_native_distill_dataset (action freely generated by the teacher -- contributes real
    action-loss gradient).
  - kd_static_500m_std_0_05_action_syn_tn_pa.yaml: synthetic_dataset_dir =
    kd_synthetic_distill_dataset_merged_std_0_05 (teacher-native MERGED with the perturbed-action/
    counterfactual data).
train_kd_static_action.py's loss (combined_kd_loss_action_only) supervises ONLY the action slot,
for both the real and synthetic terms -- there is no value or future-state loss term in this
variant's objective at all. So unlike av_syn_tn_pa/syn_tn_pa (where the perturbed-action examples'
~0 action-gradient is a real tradeoff, paid for by value/future-state supervision those variants DO
have), action_syn_tn_pa has nothing to collect that payoff with: roughly half its synthetic batch
each step (the perturbed-action half) contributes ~0 gradient to the only loss term this variant
computes, for zero compensating benefit. This is a more direct explanation for action_syn_tn_pa's
trailing action loss than "harder optimization target" -- for this one variant specifically, the
mechanism the merged dataset is designed around doesn't apply to what's being trained, so the
merge is close to pure dilution rather than a signal/coverage tradeoff.

Not yet checked: whether this same "no payoff" issue applies differently to av_syn_tn_pa (which
DOES have a value loss term, so the counterfactual examples' value target is usable) vs. syn_tn_pa
(which also has future-state loss, so both value and future-state targets are usable) -- i.e.
whether the size of the _pa performance penalty on action_loss scales with how much of the
counterfactual signal each variant's objective can actually use. Worth checking value_loss/
future_*_loss deltas (not just action_loss) between _pa and non-_pa for av_syn_tn/av_syn_tn_pa and
syn_tn/syn_tn_pa specifically, to see if the perturbation is paying off where it's actually
supposed to for those two variants, before concluding anything about whether _pa is worth keeping
in the sweep at all.

============================================================
2026-08-18 (later still) -- REVISION: the "rg31609-specific" silent-crash diagnosis from earlier
today was also wrong -- same signature now reproduced on a 3rd, different node
============================================================

syn_tn_pa (job 19207884, one of the 6 relaunched-at-1000-iter-frequency eval jobs) produced its
first post-relaunch row at iter 13000: `failed`, exit -6 (SIGABRT), no traceback in
eval_failure_iter_000013000.log -- checked the log directly: crash is mid-rollout on task "pick up
the alphabet soup and place it in the basket", episode 3 of 3, cut off after the t=234 INFO line,
identical to the original syn_tn/rg31609 crash signature from earlier today. Job landed on
**rg21807** -- not rg31609, and not previously implicated at all. Confirmed $HOME quota is fine
(476K/500K, flat, ruling that explanation back out too).

This is the third node (rg31609 x2, now rg21807) to produce this exact signature, and the pattern
across all occurrences to date is: same task (alphabet_soup), same episode (3rd), same seed (7,
non-randomized), same timestep (t=234) -- reproducing across different nodes, different variants
(syn_tn, action_syn_tn, now syn_tn_pa), and different model checkpoints including iter_000000000
(untrained init weights). The one thing that has NEVER varied across any occurrence is the exact
simulated scenario itself. This makes "node-specific" (the theory the 2026-08-17 entries settled
on, and the reason rg31609 was being excluded) no longer well-supported -- a bug in the LIBERO/
MuJoCo sim itself for this one specific deterministic rollout (task/episode/seed/timestep
combination) fits the evidence better than any node/GPU/quota explanation tried so far.

Practical impact is small and non-blocking: iter 13000 for syn_tn_pa is now permanently marked
`failed` (won't be retried, same load_already_evaluated() behavior noted earlier) and the job
continues normally to iter 14000+. No action taken yet.

Next: if this recurs again (a 4th occurrence, on any node, for any variant) it's worth trying to
reproduce the alphabet_soup/episode-3/seed-7/t=234 rollout standalone (outside the periodic-eval
subprocess wrapper) to get a real traceback instead of the silent SIGABRT the subprocess capture
currently produces -- that would confirm or rule out the sim-bug theory directly rather than
inferring it from recurrence patterns. Should also stop excluding rg31609 by default in future
submissions (per the 2026-08-17 entry's suggestion) since node exclusion is no longer the
best-supported mitigation.

--- 2nd occurrence within minutes, on a 4th distinct node -- theory now well-supported, not
just plausible ---

av_syn_tn (job 19207885) failed identically at its own first post-relaunch checkpoint, also iter
13000: exit -6, task=alphabet_soup, episode 3, cut off after the t=234 INFO line -- landed on
rg21803, a 4th node (rg31609 x2, rg21807, now rg21803). The matching iteration number (13000) across
syn_tn_pa and av_syn_tn is coincidental, not a clue -- both jobs simply happened to be at similar
pre-relaunch progress (12400/12500), so 13000 was each one's own first post-relaunch multiple of
1000, nothing iteration-specific. Of the 3 relaunched eval jobs that have started running so far,
2/3 hit this crash on their very first checkpoint. The 3rd (syn_tn, on a 5th distinct node
rg31603) is still mid-episode as of this writing, not yet resolved either way.

This is now 4 independent reproductions (4 nodes, 4 variants across syn_tn/action_syn_tn/syn_tn_pa/
av_syn_tn, multiple checkpoints including iter 0) with the sim-bug theory the only explanation that
fits every occurrence. Still non-blocking (jobs continue past the one skipped checkpoint each time)
but the 2/3 immediate-hit rate on this batch of relaunches is high enough that if it keeps
recurring at this rate, the missed-checkpoint count could start being a real gap in the
success-rate curves rather than a rare footnote -- worth prioritizing the standalone-repro
investigation (see "Next" above) sooner rather than later if the pattern continues.

--- Mitigation: sim-crash resume ---

SIGABRT cannot be caught in-process. Added out-of-process resume:
  - run_libero_eval.py: --eval_progress_path JSON (completed[] + in_progress); skip already
    recorded episodes on relaunch; clear file after a clean full-suite finish.
  - kd/eval_sim_crash_resume.py: relaunch on exit -6/134; mark in_progress episode as
    success=False / reason=sim_crash (counts as a failed trial in the 30-episode denominator);
    max_restarts default raised to 25 after verification showed 5 was too low for multi-landmine
    policies.
  - Wired into periodic_libero_eval_static.run_eval and kd_static_final_ckpt_eval.sbatch
    (sbatch also replaces any existing iteration=59999 CSV row so a prior failed attempt is not
    permanent; walltime 08:00:00 to allow multiple model reloads).

Crashed episodes are recorded as 0 success (failure), not excluded from the denominator.

Investigation note (same evening): crash is NOT from NaN/Inf actions. Logged actions are finite;
slight OOB vs dataset gripper ±1 is common on both crashing and surviving policies; env-only
no-op repro through all 30 episodes does not crash. Crash is policy-action-sequence-dependent
(MuJoCo hard-abort on bad contact/constraint during env.step).

============================================================
2026-08-25 -- chasing the "reach nearest object" failure: data, steps, conditioning
============================================================

Context: published the final-checkpoint dashboard (all 6 variants, iter 59999, rollout videos
included) as a Claude Artifact and reviewed it there. Confirms the 2026-08-18 finding above (lines
~481-484: every arm reaches toward whichever object is nearest/leftmost, not the named target) still
holds at the final checkpoint, uniformly across all 6 variants -- not something more training alone
fixed.

--- Ruled out: success-only data filtering as the cause ---

Hypothesis: since training data is filtered to success-only replays (regenerate_libero_dataset.py
keeps only done==True episodes), maybe a demonstrator that fails more often on non-nearest-object
reaches biases the kept set toward "nearest = target", teaching a shortcut instead of real
conditioning.

Checked the actual libero_object_metainfo.json: 455/500 (91.0%) kept, per-task range 84-100%,
fairly even across all 10 target objects -- no task lost dramatically more than others. Drop rate
looks like ordinary sim-replay nondeterminism (physics jitter), not a competence gap tied to object
position. Hypothesis not supported by the numbers; not pursuing further.

--- Ruled out (for now): step count / training budget ---

max_iter=60000 was already the full configured budget for these runs, deliberately sized (see
build_distill_dataset_std_0.05.yaml's own comment) so the 16k-real + 16k-synthetic pool gets
revisited ~15x, matching the live KD path's natural repetition rate over its ~470-episode demo pool
-- not an accidental early stop.

Pulled the dashboard's own loss/success arrays directly (not re-derived): action loss for every
variant drops sharply by ~10% of training and is essentially flat for the back half (e.g. syn_tn:
0.0167 @10% -> 0.0048 @50% -> 0.0035 @90% -> 0.0045 final). Eval success over the last 10 evaluated
checkpoints (iters 51000-59999) is flat 0% for 5/6 variants; syn_tn's one 3.3% point is an isolated
blip with zero on either side, not a trend. Loss plateaus around iter 20-30k while success never
moves at all -- more steps of this same recipe would not be expected to help; the loss floor is
already being hit for 30k+ iterations with zero corresponding task-success signal.

--- Correction: which eval pipeline these checkpoints actually use ---

Caught mid-investigation: an earlier pass (via a subagent) checked cosmos_policy/scripts/simple_dit/
eval.py's build_proprio/normalize/flip logic for a pipeline bug and found none -- but that's the
WRONG module. These 6 variants are cosmos_kd_student_500m_libero DiT students
(kd_static_500m_std_0_05_{syn_tn,syn_tn_pa,av_syn_tn,av_syn_tn_pa,action_syn_tn,action_syn_tn_pa})
evaluated via kd/periodic_libero_eval_static.py -> run_libero_eval.py/cosmos_utils.py, confirmed by
job_name/checkpoint-iteration/eval-suite-size (10 tasks x 3 trials = 30 episodes) all matching the
kd_static_*_eval.yaml configs exactly. The simple_dit pipeline is unrelated to this sweep. Treat the
earlier "no eval-pipeline bug" conclusion as unconfirmed for the actual code path in use.

--- Conditioning wiring: checked clean, but doesn't rule out attention collapse ---

Reused the team's own .kd_verify_scratch/inspect_condition_shapes.py run from 2026-08-11: text
conditioning goes in via real cross-attention (crossattn_emb, shape (B, 512, 1024), from T5 text
embeddings) onto the teacher-inherited architecture, not a fragile additive sum -- and
kd/distill_dataset_test.py confirms crossattn_emb round-trips through the KD dataset pipeline
untouched (no dropout/masking bug). So the *wiring* is correct. That doesn't rule out the student
having learned to ignore language in practice (attention collapse) despite correct wiring -- wiring
being fine and behavior being fine are different claims.

--- New diagnostic: does swapping ONLY the instruction change the student's action? ---

Wrote .kd_verify_scratch/probe_text_conditioning.py (+ matching .sbatch launcher, same header
pattern as kd_verify_final.sbatch, 2x H100 / 30 min). For a handful of real (image, proprio)
examples, runs the student's denoiser twice with identical xt/sigma (same torch.manual_seed feeding
teacher.training_step, which builds those) but two different T5 instruction embeddings pulled
straight from t5_embeddings.pkl, and compares the resulting action difference against the
noise-floor difference (same instruction, two different noise draws). Ratio near 1x => student is
ignoring language (conditioning collapse); ratio well above 1x => language is having a real effect
and the nearest-object bias needs a different explanation.

Verified all input paths exist before submitting (checkpoint iter_000059999/model.pt under
.../cosmos_dit_wm_output/cosmos_policy/cosmos_v2_finetune/
cosmos_distill_experiments_kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn/checkpoints/, dataset dir, t5_embeddings.pkl
all present). Submitted:

  job_id     script                              probing
  19575677   probe_text_conditioning.sbatch       syn_tn, iter 59999

Next: read .kd_verify_scratch/probe_text_conditioning_19575677.out once it completes. If it shows
conditioning collapse, that points at the training objective/recipe (student never learns to attend
to text within its budget) rather than more data or more steps. If it shows language having a real
effect, revisit the baseline_1b_train (no-KD) comparison next to isolate whether this is a
KD-specific artifact. Worth re-running the same probe against a second variant (e.g. action_syn_tn,
the only other one with a training-loss edge) if syn_tn's result is ambiguous.

--- 19575677 CRASHED (device bug in the probe script, not a finding) ---

FAILED after ~1 min, exit code 1: `RuntimeError: Expected all tensors to be on the same device, but
got tensors is on cuda:1, different from other tensors on cuda:0` inside
minimal_v4_dit.py:prepare_embedded_sequence's torch.cat. Root cause: the student's forward pass
reads its compute device via torch.cuda.current_device(), not the input tensors' device -- teacher
ops just before it leave current_device=cuda:0, so the student call needs an explicit
`with torch.cuda.device(student_device):` pin. This is a known, previously-hit issue --
identity_kd_test.py:64-66 already documents and applies the identical fix; probe_text_conditioning.py
had missed it (offline_eval.py, which the script's pattern was based on, appears to have the same
gap, unexercised). Fixed by wrapping the `student.denoise(...)` call in
`torch.cuda.device(student_device)`. Resubmitted:

  job_id     script                              probing
  19576419   probe_text_conditioning.sbatch       syn_tn, iter 59999

--- 19576419 COMPLETED -- language conditioning is real but weak, not collapsed ---

  [n]  embed_cosine_sim   action_diff(lang swap)   action_diff(noise floor)   ratio
  0    0.29               0.0547                   0.0309                     1.77x
  1    0.29               0.0396                   0.0327                     1.21x
  2    0.26               0.0463                   0.0218                     2.13x
  3    0.26               0.0496                   0.0468                     1.06x
  4    0.67               0.1030                   0.0632                     1.63x
  5    0.81               0.1178                   0.0132                     8.90x
  6    0.30               0.0728                   0.0294                     2.47x
  7    1.00               0.0050                   0.0443                     0.11x  <- own/swapped
                                                                                  embedding were
                                                                                  IDENTICAL (dataset
                                                                                  example's true
                                                                                  instruction happened
                                                                                  to match the fixed
                                                                                  "swap" command) --
                                                                                  not a real language
                                                                                  swap, exclude from
                                                                                  the read

Script-reported summary (includes the [7] non-swap): mean ratio 1.73x. Excluding [7] (the only
non-swap): 7 real swaps, ratio range 1.06x-8.90x, mean ~2.7x -- consistently above 1x but for half
the examples only marginally so (1.06x-1.63x), with one large outlier (8.90x) pulling the mean up.

Read: language conditioning is NOT collapsed (a true collapse would sit at/near 1x across the
board, like [7] does) -- swapping the instruction reliably moves the output beyond the noise floor.
But the effect is inconsistent and often weak (several examples barely above noise), not the strong,
confident signal you'd expect from a policy that reliably discriminates its target by instruction.
This fits a middle ground: the student has learned *some* language sensitivity, just not enough to
reliably override whatever's driving "reach for the nearest object" -- more consistent with
under-trained/weakly-integrated conditioning than with a wiring bug or full attention collapse.
Combined with the loss-plateau finding above (action loss flat for the last 30k+ iters while success
stays at 0%), the picture is: this checkpoint has weak-but-nonzero task conditioning, and continuing
to train the current recipe for more steps is not expected to strengthen it (loss has nothing left
to give under this objective/data).

Next: the baseline_1b_train (no-KD) comparison is now the more informative next move -- it would
show whether standard (non-distilled) training on the same data develops materially stronger
language conditioning, which would point the fix at the KD objective/recipe specifically rather than
at data scale or model capacity in general. Also worth re-running this same probe (now fixed) against
action_syn_tn (the only other variant with a training-loss edge) to check whether conditioning
strength differs meaningfully across the 6 objectives, or whether ~weak-but-real is uniform across
all of them like the success-rate flatline was.

across at least these two variants -- weak-but-nonzero, not collapsed, not strong -- consistent with
this being a shared property of the training recipe/data rather than something one KD loss variant
fixed and another didn't. Reinforces that the baseline_1b_train comparison is the right next move
(isolates recipe-shared vs. KD-specific) rather than probing more of the remaining 4 variants, which
would likely just replicate this same pattern.

--- baseline_1b_train prep: checked launch-readiness before committing a 12h GPU job ---

Before submitting, verified the two things baseline_1b_train.yaml's own comments flag as needing
confirmation:
  1. kd_inits/student_init_1b.pt does not exist yet on cluster storage (only student_init_500m.pt is
     there) -- init_student_from_teacher.py --student_size 1b has never been run.
  2. Even once generated, it would likely NOT be loaded: baseline_1b_train.yaml goes through the
     standard torchrun/DistributedCheckpointer path (unlike kd_static, which loads its student init
     via its own code), and DistributedCheckpointer.keys_to_resume_during_load() (dcp.py:493) has
     `if self.load_path and not str(self.load_path).endswith(".pt")` -- a .pt-suffixed load_path is
     EXCLUDED from that branch, falling through to checkpoint_path=None, i.e. training from scratch
     instead of from the teacher-derived init. init_student_from_teacher.py only ever writes a plain
     .pt via torch.save (no DCP-dir output option) -- so this isn't a config typo, it needs new
     save-format code to actually fix.

--- Building the eval companion (needed by both baseline runs) ---

periodic_libero_eval_static.py (the kd_static eval script) hardcoded `--ckpt_path=
{checkpoint_path}/model.pt` -- correct for train_kd_static.py's own flat-.pt-per-iteration format,
but WRONG for baseline_1b_train[_scratch]'s checkpoints, which go through the standard torchrun/
DistributedCheckpointer path and land as DCP-sharded directories (checkpoints/iter_NNNNNNNNN/model/
{.metadata,__0_0.distcp}, confirmed by inspecting an existing torchrun-run's checkpoints, e.g.
cosmos_dit_wm_tiny_libero_from_scratch) -- there is no model.pt file to point at.

Fix: rather than duplicate periodic_libero_eval_static.py (~200 lines, and its sim-crash-resume/
restart-safety machinery is exactly what both new runs need too), added a `checkpoint_format`
parameter (CLI: --checkpoint_format, default "kd_static" so all 12 existing kd_static run configs
are byte-for-byte unaffected) that branches the --ckpt_path construction: "kd_static" keeps the old
`{checkpoint_path}/model.pt`; "dcp" passes `{checkpoint_path}` bare, letting
load_model_from_checkpoint's own extension-based auto-detection (model_loader.py, confirmed it
"Supports both .pt and DCP checkpoint formats") route it through the DCP loader, which appends
"model" internally itself (dcp.py:549) -- exactly matching how DistributedCheckpointer.load() reads
the "model" key during training resume, already verified working via the 19598004 smoketest above.
Added matching RunConfig.eval_checkpoint_format field (sweep.py, default "kd_static", same
backward-compat reasoning) and threaded it through submit_sweep.py's kd_static_eval command
construction.

Created baseline_1b_train_eval.yaml and baseline_1b_train_scratch_eval.yaml (run_type:
kd_static_eval, eval_checkpoint_format: dcp, same 10-task x 3-trial = 30-episode libero_object
coverage as the 6 KD variants' eval, so success rates are genuinely comparable), registered in
config.yaml.

Next: wait for baseline_1b_train's first checkpoint (checkpoint_save_iter=200) to land, then verify

which is a different code path in run_libero_eval.py/cosmos_utils.py -- don't assume the training-
side verification transfers) before trusting the eval companion to run unattended. Once verified,
launch both *_eval.yaml companions.

--- baseline_1b_train_scratch (19598223) hit severe node contention -- cancelled, resubmitted ---

~55 min into monitoring, iteration counts had diverged wildly: baseline_1b_train at iter 1023 (5
checkpoints) vs. baseline_1b_train_scratch at iter 20 (0 checkpoints) over the same wall-clock
window. Confirmed via each run's own iter_speed callback (not just checkpoint counts) that this was
real, not a fluke: baseline_1b_train steady at 3.5-3.7s/iteration; baseline_1b_train_scratch steady
at 156-180s/iteration -- ~43x slower, both measured over hundreds/dozens of consecutive iterations.
Same architecture, same batch_size=8, same dataset, same num_workers=0 data-loading path -- compute
cost per iteration should be near-identical regardless of init, so this was not explained by the
scratch/random-init training dynamics themselves.

Root cause, confirmed via `scontrol show node` + `squeue -w <node>`: Slurm placed the two jobs on
different physical nodes with very different contention -- rg31701 (baseline_1b_train, fast):
CPULoad=3.13, lightly shared. rg31903 (baseline_1b_train_scratch, slow): CPULoad=22.05, also hosting
3 other users' unrelated jobs at the time (a Jupyter spawner, a pmr-prod job, others). Since this
training path's dataloading is synchronous/CPU-bound (num_workers=0, the shared torchrun launch
path's own setting -- not something introduced for this run), heavy CPU contention on the host
directly stalls each iteration even though the GPU itself isn't the bottleneck. At the observed
rate, the run would have reached only ~270/30000 target iterations by its 12h walltime -- not enough
for a meaningful comparison point.

Asked how to proceed; told to cancel and resubmit (only ~1hr/22 iterations lost, no checkpoints yet
so nothing to preserve). Cancelled 19598223, resubmitted the same unmodified baseline_1b_train_
scratch.yaml:

  job_id     result
  19601996   baseline_1b_train_scratch, re-submitted 2026-08-25 ~10:31, hoping for a quieter node
             (not guaranteed -- Slurm placement is whatever's available at submission time)

The existing eval companion (19598636, monitors job_name=cosmos_kd_baseline_1b_train_scratch) did
NOT need restarting -- same job_name, same run_dir, it'll pick up checkpoints from 19601996 once
they land, same as if training had simply been slow rather than restarted.

Next: confirm 19601996 lands on a node with reasonable CPULoad and iter_speed comparable to
baseline_1b_train's ~3.5-3.7s/iteration (not another ~150s+/iteration draw) before trusting it to
run unattended for the full 12h. If it lands on another contended node again, consider whether to
just accept a slower run, request more exclusive resources, or reduce num_workers=0's exposure to
contention some other way -- not yet decided, only relevant if this recurs.

Asked whether the 93.3% jump is explained by dropping KD (loss-type) or by the size difference
(baseline_1b_train is ~988M/14 blocks; all 6 KD variants are ~500M/7 blocks) -- checked: no
kd_static_1b_* run exists anywhere in conf/runs/, and no baseline_500m_train (non-KD at 500M)

    KD variants use, from the same teacher-derived init they use -- isolates loss-type at fixed size.

    500M variant, kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn) at the SAME ~1B size baseline_1b_train uses --
    isolates size at fixed loss-type.

--- Monitoring pass (~22:35): 19636971 wasn't slow, it FAILED (EADDRINUSE) -- real bug, fixed ---

19636971 wasn't in squeue at all at this check -- sacct showed FAILED, 00:00:06, exit 1:0. Root
cause in the .err: torchrun's rendezvous TCPStore failed to bind port 12341 --
`torch.distributed.DistNetworkError: ... EADDRINUSE`. Not node contention: submit_sweep.py's
build_sbatch_script() hardcodes `--master_port=12341` for EVERY torchrun run_type, and Slurm had
packed 19636971 onto rg31703 -- the SAME physical node baseline_1b_train_scratch (19601996) was
already running on, also via torchrun, also on port 12341. Two single-GPU torchrun jobs sharing a
node with the same fixed port is a real, reproducible collision, not a resource-speed problem
"resubmit and hope" would reliably dodge (Slurm can and does pack multiple of this user's 1-GPU jobs
onto one multi-GPU node, as seen with rg31703 hosting both baseline_1b_train_scratch and, briefly,
this run).

Fix: added `master_port: int = 12341` to RunConfig (sweep.py, default matches the prior hardcoded
value so every other existing yaml is unaffected), and changed submit_sweep.py's torchrun cmd
string to use `run.master_port` instead of the literal. Gave baseline_500m_train.yaml master_port:
12343 and baseline_1b_train_scratch.yaml master_port: 12342 (baseline_1b_train.yaml keeps the
12341 default) -- the 3 torchrun-based runs in this sweep now each own a distinct port, so no pair
of them can collide regardless of how Slurm places them. Dry-ran submit_sweep.py first to confirm
the new port appears correctly in the generated sbatch's torchrun invocation before submitting.

--- New probe: does the TEACHER itself struggle with libero_object, independent of KD? ---

Motivation (from this session's discussion, not yet in this file): reason #1 for "KD loss looks
good but success doesn't follow" -- video-reconstruction terms drowning out the action-relevant
gradient -- was ruled out, since action_syn_tn/action_syn_tn_pa (run_type: kd_static_action,
confirmed via their yaml headers) strip those terms out entirely and STILL flatlined at 0% final
success with the same weak-but-real conditioning signature as syn_tn. That leaves open whether the
ceiling is a KD-recipe/distillation problem, or whether the released TEACHER itself (nvidia/
Cosmos-Policy-LIBERO-Predict2-2B, cosmos_predict2_2b_480p_libero__inference_only) is only weakly
discriminative across libero_object's 10 near-identical "pick up X, place in basket" instructions --
in which case KD would just be faithfully distilling a mediocre teacher, and no student-side loss
change would fix it. No existing journal entry checks the teacher's own success rate directly.

run_libero_eval.py's own module docstring documents a direct-teacher-eval recipe ("*** Main
checkpoint: 98.5% success rate ***", lines 39-72) -- --ckpt_path/--dataset_stats_path/
--t5_text_embeddings_path all point at the nvidia/Cosmos-Policy-LIBERO-Predict2-2B HF repo directly
(auto-resolved via cosmos_utils.py's snapshot_download/hf_hub_download -- no local checkpoint prep
needed, unlike every student init in this file). Deliberately used the teacher's OWN bundled
dataset-stats/T5-embeddings rather than this repo's regenerated libero_object_regen ones -- action
unnormalization is checkpoint-specific, and pairing the teacher with stats it wasn't calibrated
against risks a spurious low score with nothing to do with language conditioning (noted as a real
methodology tradeoff: this run's normalization source differs from every other eval in this file).
task_suite_name=libero_object, num_trials_per_task=3 (10 tasks x 3 trials = 30 episodes) to match
every other run's own coverage for a comparable-scale number, even though the stats-file source
differs.

Verified before committing to a long job: 1-task/1-trial smoketest first (job 19638408, COMPLETED
00:01:08) -- confirmed the HF-hub download path actually works from a compute node (no cached
local assets existed yet: "Local assets not found. Downloading from HuggingFace Hub..."), the
checkpoint loads, and a real rollout runs end-to-end (task 0/alphabet soup, Success: True, 100%).
Launched the real 30-episode run:

  job_id     purpose                          result
  19638421   teacher_eval_libero_object        RUNNING, launched 2026-08-25 ~23:23. .kd_verify_
                                                scratch/teacher_eval_libero_object.sbatch, 3h limit.

Next: read teacher_eval_libero_object_19638421.out once it completes -- overall + per-task success
rate on libero_object. If the teacher itself scores low/inconsistently across the 10 objects (like
the KD students), that reframes the whole investigation toward the teacher/pretraining, not the
distillation recipe. If the teacher scores high (matching its published ~98.5%), that rules the
teacher out and puts the blame squarely back on the KD objective/recipe/training budget.

             tasks at 3/3 (1.0) -- including task 5 (tomato sauce), the ONE task baseline_1b_train's

This definitively rules out "the teacher itself is only weakly discriminative on libero_object" as
an explanation for the 6 KD variants' 3.3%-or-worse ceiling (raised earlier this session, not
previously in this file). The teacher is not mediocre on this task family -- it's essentially
perfect, on the SAME 10-task x 3-trial coverage every other eval in this file uses (methodology
caveat still applies: this run used the teacher's own bundled dataset-stats/T5-embeddings rather
than this repo's regenerated ones, per the entry above -- but a 100%-vs-~0% gap is far too large to
be explained by a normalization difference). Puts the blame for the KD variants' failure squarely
back on the distillation recipe/objective/training budget, not the teacher's own competence -- KD is
failing to transfer capability that demonstrably exists in the teacher.

--- Active hypotheses being tested by the 4 running pairs (consolidated summary) ---

Four live threads, one per pair, plus one just resolved:

1. SIZE vs. KD-LOSS CONFOUND (the reason baseline_500m_train/kd_static_1b_std_0_05_syn_tn exist at
   all -- see this file's ~22:35 "Status check" entry): baseline_1b_train (1B, non-KD) hit 93.3%; the
   6 KD variants (500M, KD) hit at best 3.3% -- but no prior run isolated size from loss-type, so the
   93.3%-vs-3.3% gap couldn't be attributed to either one specifically.
     - baseline_500m_train / baseline_500m_train_eval: does non-KD training AT THE SAME 500M size the
       KD variants use also reach high success, or does it stay near their 3.3% ceiling? High ->
       dropping KD is what matters, not size. Low -> size matters too. Status: iter ~980/30000, eval
       still 0.0% at iter 400 -- too early to read.
     - kd_static_1b_std_0_05_syn_tn / its eval: does KD training AT THE SAME 1B size baseline_1b_train
       uses close the gap toward 93%, or stay stuck near 3%? Closes the gap -> KD's problem was mostly
       capacity/size. Stays stuck -> the recipe itself is the bottleneck regardless of size. Status:
       checkpoints at iter ~3800/60000, eval only through iter 300, all 0.0% -- long way to go, this
       is the slowest-progressing comparison (eval throughput-bound, see the ~16h-to-iter-4000
       estimate discussed this session, not yet written to this file in exact form).

2. DOES THE TEACHER-DERIVED INIT MATTER AT ALL for standard (non-KD) training? (baseline_1b_train_
   scratch.yaml's own stated purpose, see its file header) -- baseline_1b_train (teacher-init) vs.
   baseline_1b_train_scratch (random-init), same architecture/data/update-count/loss otherwise.
   Converge to similar success -> init isn't doing much for non-KD training. Scratch lags
   persistently -> init matters. Status: scratch only just became healthy this session (~23:36 entry,
   after most of its wall-clock time lost to node contention/a 12h TIMEOUT) -- iter ~810/30000, eval
   still 0.0% at iter 400. Effectively just starting for real.

3. RESOLVED THIS SESSION -- was the teacher itself only weakly conditioned on libero_object? NO
   (19638421 entry above): teacher scored 100% (30/30), including the one task baseline_1b_train's
   own checkpoint got wrong. Rules out "mediocre teacher" as the explanation for the KD variants'
   ceiling; points the blame at the KD objective/recipe/training budget instead.

4. BACKGROUND HYPOTHESIS this all feeds (from this session's discussion of "why is KD loss bad as a
   proxy", not a running job -- covered by reasoning already in this file re: action_syn_tn ruling
   out video-reconstruction dominance, plus the probe_text_conditioning.py findings on weak-but-real
   conditioning): KD's training loss crashes toward zero while eval success stays flat, and with the
   teacher now confirmed competent (point 3), that gap looks like a genuine distillation-transfer
   failure -- the student isn't learning to reproduce behavior the teacher demonstrably has -- rather
   than a capability ceiling inherited from a weak teacher or diluted by irrelevant loss terms.

Once (1) and (2) have enough real data to read, the intended synthesis is: does non-KD training win
regardless of size (favors "KD recipe is the problem"), does KD catch up at 1B (favors "KD just
needed more capacity"), and does the init matter for a well-behaved non-KD run at all (a separate,
narrower question about this specific teacher-derived init's value).

--- New probe: does a KD checkpoint's future-frame prediction actually match reality, given the
    REAL action? (arm rendering is the weak point, not the static scene) ---

Motivation: the "why is KD loss bad as a proxy" discussion (this session, not previously in this
file) argued KD's low training loss doesn't guarantee an accurate world model. Wanted a direct,
visual check: take a real successful demo, and compare (a) the real environment frames as its real
actions execute against (b) a KD checkpoint's own predicted future frame when conditioned on that
SAME real (unperturbed) action -- isolating world-model/future-frame quality from action-selection
quality entirely (the model is never asked to choose an action here, only to predict a consequence).

Built entirely as a NEW standalone script + sbatch (`.kd_verify_scratch/probe_future_frame_
prediction.py`/`.sbatch`) -- no existing file touched, per explicit instruction not to risk the 8
live training/eval jobs. Reuses existing, already-proven machinery rather than reimplementing
anything: `kd/synthetic_generation.py`'s `generate_synthetic_targets(model, batch,
action_perturbation_std=0.0, ...)` (already built for `build_synthetic_distill_dataset.py`, just
called here with ZERO perturbation = the exact recorded action held fixed, generating future
image/proprio/value via the model's own real multi-step `generate_samples_from_batch` sampling, NOT
the training-time single-denoise shortcut) and `LIBERODataset` itself (`use_image_aug=False` for
clean ground truth) for the real demo's windowed (chunk_size=16) action chunks. Ground-truth frames
come straight from the regenerated demo's own HDF5 (`dataset._load_demo_episode_images`, bypassing
`__getitem__`'s preprocessing) -- these are literally what a real env replay produced, no fresh sim
re-run needed since regenerate_libero_dataset.py already did that once to build libero_object_regen.

Target checkpoint: kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn/checkpoints/iter_000059999 -- the flatlined,
best-of-the-6 KD variant (3.3% final success) this whole "why doesn't KD work" thread is about.
Demo: episode_idx=0, task="pick up the chocolate pudding and place it in the basket", 168 steps ->
11 windows. num_denoising_steps=1 (matches real eval's own default, not an idealized best case).

  job_id     result
  19650960   probe_future_frame_prediction, COMPLETED 00:01:01, exit 0. Produced 3 videos under
             .kd_verify_scratch/future_frame_probe_kd500m/: env_execution_full.mp4 (full real
             trajectory), model_future_predictions.mp4 and env_future_at_windows.mp4 (11
             time-matched anchor-point pairs for direct comparison).

Inspected 3 sample windows (0, 5, 10) as extracted PNG stills. Clear, consistent pattern across all
three: the STATIC scene (table, the 5 sauce/food items, the basket) is predicted with near
pixel-perfect accuracy against the real frame in every window -- the checkpoint's world-model
prediction is genuinely good here. The ROBOT ARM, however, is consistently rendered as a blurry,
under-resolved, hazy blob (worst at the wrist/gripper) in all 3 windows -- including window 10,
where the real arm has clearly moved to a new, distinct pose (reaching over the basket) that the
prediction only vaguely approximates. This is a real, visually-grounded lead for "why KD loss looks
good but success doesn't follow": the checkpoint learned to predict the (larger-area, lower-variance)
static background sharply, but never learned to sharply resolve its own dynamic end-effector state --
exactly the part of the scene most load-bearing for knowing where the gripper is relative to the
target object, which would degrade both value estimation and action selection even if the rest of
the scene is well-modeled.

Next: worth running this same probe against (a) a non-KD checkpoint (baseline_1b_train or
baseline_500m_train, once further along) for comparison -- does non-KD training resolve the arm
sharply where KD doesn't, or is blurry-arm-prediction a shared property of this architecture/data
regardless of loss-type; (b) the teacher itself, given its own 100% eval result -- if even the
teacher's future-frame prediction blurs the arm, this is a shared world-model limitation, not
something KD specifically introduced; (c) more windows/demos than just one 168-step trajectory, to
check whether this is representative or specific to this one demo/task.

--- Follow-up (a)+(b) run: blurry-arm is NOT KD-specific, and does NOT predict task failure --
    revises the leading hypothesis ---

Same probe, same demo (episode_idx=0, "pick up the chocolate pudding..."), same script (no edits --
just two new sbatch launchers pointing at different checkpoints), against the teacher and against
baseline_1b_train's live checkpoint (iter_000019200, read-only snapshot -- does not touch the still-
training job):

  job_id     target                                          result
  19651250   teacher (nvidia/Cosmos-Policy-LIBERO-Predict2-2B) COMPLETED 00:00:55, exit 0
  19651251   baseline_1b_train iter_000019200 (non-KD, 1B)     COMPLETED 00:00:41, exit 0

Inspected the same windows (0, 10) as stills for both:

  target                                    task success    arm rendering
  teacher (2B, 28 blocks)                   100%            SHARP, closely tracks the real pose
  baseline_1b_train (988M, 14 blocks, non-KD) ~90-96%        BLURRY/hazy -- same character as the
                                                              KD checkpoint's blur
  kd_static_500m_std_0_05_syn_tn (503M, 7 blocks, KD)  3.3%  BLURRY/hazy

This REVISES the leading hypothesis from the prior entry, doesn't confirm it: baseline_1b_train has
the SAME blurry-arm rendering as the failing KD checkpoint, yet succeeds at the task ~90-96% of the
time. So blurry future-frame arm-rendering is (1) NOT specific to KD -- only the full-size teacher
renders it sharply; every depth-reduced student tried so far (KD or non-KD) blurs it -- and (2) NOT
predictive of task failure on its own -- a model can have a visually blurry self-model and still act
correctly, meaning the action output isn't simply "read off" the predicted image's visual fidelity.
Reduced depth (14 or 7 blocks vs. the teacher's 28) plausibly just isn't enough capacity to render
a small, fast-moving, self-occluding object (the robot's own arm) sharply in image space, regardless
of loss-type -- but the DIFFUSION MODEL's internal action-relevant representation can apparently
still be good enough for real task success even when its own visualized self-model is blurry.

Net effect on the original question ("why doesn't KD work"): this particular lead is weaker than it
first looked. The gap between baseline_1b_train's ~93% and the KD variants' 3.3% is NOT explained by
arm-rendering fidelity (both blur it, only one succeeds) -- redirects back toward the KD training
dynamics themselves (recipe/data/budget, per the loss-is-a-bad-proxy reasoning already in this file)
rather than world-model visual fidelity as the driver. Videos saved under .kd_verify_scratch/
future_frame_probe_teacher/ and future_frame_probe_baseline1b/ if useful for further inspection.
