--- New probe: does the model's OWN chosen action match the real demo action? (concrete lead: KD's
    gripper/vertical-position error spikes exactly where it counts) ---

Motivation: every probe so far measured FUTURE-FRAME prediction quality (world-model fidelity), not
whether the model's own ACTION choice is accurate. Since baseline_1b_train has the same blurry-arm
world-model issue as the failing KD checkpoint yet still succeeds (prior entry), action quality
itself was still unmeasured. Built `.kd_verify_scratch/probe_action_accuracy.py` (new standalone
script, no existing file touched) -- same demo, same 11 non-overlapping chunk_size=16 windows, same
non-autoregressive discipline (every window's input state is the REAL recorded state from
`dataset[start]`, never a prior window's output) as probe_future_frame_prediction.py, but calls
`generate_teacher_native_targets` instead of `generate_synthetic_targets` -- this lets the model
freely GENERATE its own action chunk for each real state (nothing held fixed), which is then
compared against the real recorded action chunk at that same window. Both real and predicted actions
are pulled through the SAME LIBERODataset normalization (item["actions"], not the raw unnormalized
per-episode array) so the comparison is apples-to-apples in the model's own training space.

One real bug caught before trusting results: `item["actions"]` is already a numpy array (not a
torch.Tensor) -- an initial `.numpy()` call crashed the first run (job 19652832, exit 1); fixed to
`np.asarray(item["actions"])` and reran clean (job 19652943).

  job_id     target                          result
  19652943   kd_static_500m iter 59999 (KD)   COMPLETED 00:00:54, exit 0
  19653027   teacher                          COMPLETED 00:00:38, exit 0
  19653028   baseline_1b_train iter_000020200 (non-KD, read-only snapshot) COMPLETED 00:00:56, exit 0

Per-window mean|error| (7-dim normalized action space) is unremarkable and comparable across all
three models for most of the 11 windows (roughly 0.02-0.19) -- EXCEPT window start=64, where
kd_static_500m spikes to 0.41 while teacher stays at 0.085 and baseline_1b_train at 0.054 (same
window, same real state). Gripper-direction accuracy at that window: teacher 100%, baseline_1b_train
100%, kd_static_500m only 31% -- worse than a coin flip. Per-dimension breakdown at window 64 shows
the error is NOT spread evenly: kd_static_500m's gripper error is 1.419 (vs. teacher's 0.003 and
baseline_1b_train's 0.012) and its dz (vertical position) error is 0.910 (vs. 0.035 / 0.107) --
both dramatically larger than its own other 5 dimensions (0.05-0.13, unremarkable) and than either
other model's dz/gripper. This window plausibly corresponds to the grasp/lift phase of the task
(gripper close + vertical lift) -- exactly where getting gripper timing and height wrong would
directly cause task failure.

This is the most concrete, quantitative lead yet for "why doesn't KD work": it isn't a diffuse,
uniform action-quality gap -- it's localized to specific dimensions at a specific, plausibly
task-critical moment, and the world-model blur finding (shared by both students, not KD-specific)
doesn't explain it, but this DOES look student/KD-specific so far (n=1 demo, 1 window -- see Next).
Published as a Claude Artifact: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/75571af8-16d9-4f96-abf7-9b23c3429c63
(3 charts: per-window MAE, per-window gripper accuracy, per-dimension breakdown at the spike window).

Next: this is one demo, one window -- worth checking (a) whether other demos/tasks show the same
gripper/dz-specific spike pattern at their own critical (grasp/place) moments, or whether window 64
here was a one-off; (b) whether baseline_500m_train (now at 90% eval success, non-KD, same 500M size
as the failing KD checkpoint) ALSO stays accurate at its own equivalent critical windows -- if so,
that further isolates this to the KD loss/recipe specifically, not size, matching the eval-curve
finding from earlier this entry.

--- baseline_1b_train_scratch: new Claude Artifact published (~09:20, 2026-08-26) ---

Built a loss + eval-success dashboard for baseline_1b_train_scratch, matching the existing
baseline_1b_train / baseline_500m_train artifacts' format (loss curve, eval-success curve, per-task
bars at the peak checkpoint). Data as of this build: peak eval 96.7% (29/30) at iter 8,000, latest
70% (21/30) at iter 8,400, loss 0.1975 at iter 10,164 (still training, max_iter=30,000).
Published: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/0b2207fd-6f41-4854-8ccc-aa13b5f4731e

This is the third leg of the 1B init-ablation comparison (see this run's own yaml docstring):
baseline_1b_train_scratch uses a RANDOM init (no teacher-derived checkpoint.load_path), everything
else identical to baseline_1b_train (teacher-derived init). Both are now converging into a similar
high-success band (90-100% oscillating for baseline_1b_train, 90-97% peak but noisier for scratch) --
suggestive that the teacher-derived init isn't the deciding factor for non-KD training success,
though scratch is still mid-training and its own peak/oscillation pattern needs more iterations to
read confidently. Not treating this as conclusive yet.

--- kd_static_1b_std_0_05_syn_tn Artifact: fixed off-chart rendering bug (~10:15) ---

User reported the loss curve rendering outside the plot area. Root cause: the chart template's
log-scale y-axis had a hardcoded floor `Math.max(yMin * 0.9, 0.01)`, copied unchanged from the other
3 artifacts whose loss never drops below ~0.12-0.17. This run's loss converges to 0.002, well under
that 0.01 floor, so points below it got a negative (ly - lyMin) term and rendered past the bottom of
the plot. Fixed by lowering the floor to 0.0001 in this artifact's own copy of the chart-building JS
(the other 3 artifacts' loss never approaches that range, left unchanged). Republished at the same
URL: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/a94b769e-3736-4414-8582-93cd45d11c2e -- not a data or training
issue, purely a chart-rendering bug in the artifact build script.

--- kd_static_1b_scratch_std_0_05_syn_tn: BLOCKED, needs real code fix (~10:35) ---

Set out to build the 4th leg of the 2x2 (KD vs. non-KD) x (teacher-derived vs. random init) design:
KD training with a random (not teacher-derived) init, to test whether KD's failure is intrinsic to
the loss or specifically interacting with the teacher-derived init. Created
conf/runs/kd_static_1b_scratch_std_0_05_syn_tn.yaml + _eval.yaml (copies of
kd_static_1b_std_0_05_syn_tn's pair, student_init_path omitted so it defaults to ""), registered in
config.yaml (append-only, after the kd_static_1b_std_0_05_syn_tn entries).

Initially told the user this was purely additive/config-only, no code changes needed -- reasoning:
load_policy_model(checkpoint="") resolves s3_checkpoint_dir=None, and load_model_from_checkpoint
correctly skips overriding config.checkpoint.load_path when falsy (model_loader.py:90-92), so it
should fall back to cosmos_kd_student_1b_libero's own registered empty default, same mechanism as
baseline_1b_train_scratch's random init. THIS WAS WRONG -- verified by actually running a max_iter=1
smoketest (job 19658405, via submit_sweep.py Hydra CLI overrides on top of the registered run rather
than a separate yaml, FAILED in 33s):

  AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'endswith'
  ... load_model_state_dict_from_checkpoint, model_loader.py:205
  checkpoint_format = "pt" if s3_checkpoint_dir.endswith(".pt") else "dcp"

Root cause: load_model_from_checkpoint (model_loader.py:90-92) DOES correctly skip overriding
config.checkpoint.load_path when s3_checkpoint_dir is falsy, but then UNCONDITIONALLY calls
load_model_state_dict_from_checkpoint(model, config, s3_checkpoint_dir, ...) a few lines later
(inside `if not skip_load_model:`) regardless of whether s3_checkpoint_dir is None -- no matching
guard there, unlike the config.checkpoint.load_path line. The non-KD path (baseline_1b_train_scratch)
never hits this because it goes through a completely different bootstrap (cosmos_policy.scripts.
train's Trainer) that correctly guards on `if self.load_path and ...`. load_model_from_checkpoint DOES
have an escape hatch for this exact case -- `skip_load_model=True` bypasses the checkpoint-loading
block entirely, keeping the model's randomly-instantiated weights -- but train_kd_static.py's own
load_policy_model(...) call never exposes or sets it (StaticTrainParams has no such field).

Correcting the record: this is NOT purely additive after all -- a real (small, backward-compatible)
fix is needed: a new `skip_load_model: bool = False` field threaded through kd/params.py ->
sweep.py -> submit_sweep.py -> train_kd_static.py's load_policy_model(..., skip_load_model=...)
call. Default False keeps every existing config's behavior unchanged, but the fix DOES touch
train_kd_static.py/kd/params.py/sweep.py, which the currently-running kd_static_1b_std_0_05_syn_tn
job (19657242) will re-execute on its next TIMEOUT resume (~12h out) -- its own params.yaml never
sets the new field so it'd keep identical behavior, but flagged this risk-profile change explicitly
to the user rather than proceeding on the original "zero shared-code touches" premise.

User's decision: HOLD OFF on the code fix for now. kd_static_1b_scratch_std_0_05_syn_tn stays
BLOCKED/not launched -- the two new yaml files + config.yaml registration remain in place (inert,
harmless) for whenever this gets revisited. The smoketest's tiny output dir
(kd_static_1b_scratch_smoketest/) was left as-is, not cleaned up.

Next (if/when revisited): make the skip_load_model plumbing fix, re-run the max_iter=1 smoketest to
confirm a genuine random init (log should show model instantiated but no "Loaded checkpoint from..."
line), THEN launch the real 60000-iter run + eval companion.

2026-09-01 -- dependency audit + cosmos_dit_wm relocation; new task_names/task_keyword selection

--- Dependency audit: cosmos_dit_wm/ is untracked; found and fixed one real dependency before
moving it ---

Asked to move scripts/cosmos_dit_wm/ (and config/experiment/cosmos_dit_wm_experiments.py, which
lives under config/experiment/ purely so config_v2.py's blanket
import_all_modules_from_package("cosmos_policy.config.experiment", ...) sweep registers its 4
experiment names -- cosmos_dit_wm_{small,base}_libero_from_scratch (+ __inference_only)) into
draft_code/. Both are untracked in git (`git status` shows `??`, confirmed neither was ever
committed at HEAD 8b4d559), unlike everything in cosmos_distill_experiments/.

Traced every real consumer before moving anything:
  - LossCsvCallback: TWO separate classes of the same name exist -- cosmos_dit_wm's own copy, and a
    deliberate fork already living in cosmos_distill_experiments/loss_csv_callback.py (that file's
    own docstring: "Copied from ../cosmos_dit_wm/loss_csv_callback.py rather than imported from
    there, so this folder doesn't depend on a sibling directory outside itself" -- i.e. someone
    already solved this exact problem once, just not everywhere). kd/net_experiments.py (KD's own
    registration) already uses the distill_experiments copy, not cosmos_dit_wm's -- confirmed via
    diff, the two files are byte-identical except that docstring.
  - cosmos_dit_wm_experiments.py: zero real consumers anywhere outside cosmos_dit_wm's own
    submit_sweep.py/sbatch scripts (which moved with it) -- nothing in KD/distill code names any of
    its 4 registered experiments. Only reached at all via the blanket config-registration sweep.
  - run_train.py: a REAL, previously-missed dependency. 6 untracked .sbatch scripts under
    cosmos_distill_experiments/ (train.sbatch, baseline_1b_train[_scratch][_smoketest].sbatch,
    baseline_500m_train[_smoketest].sbatch) invoke it directly via
    `-m cosmos_policy.scripts.cosmos_dit_wm.run_train` -- would have broken silently on next
    regeneration/relaunch of any of these had the move gone through unfixed.

Fixed before moving: forked run_train.py into cosmos_distill_experiments/run_train.py (same
copy-not-import pattern as loss_csv_callback.py, own docstring explaining why), and -- more
importantly -- fixed the actual GENERATOR: submit_sweep.py's own sbatch-template string
(previously hardcoded `-m cosmos_policy.scripts.cosmos_dit_wm.run_train`) now emits
`-m cosmos_policy.scripts.cosmos_distill_experiments.run_train`, so a hand-sed of the generated
files alone would have been silently overwritten on next regeneration. Regenerated all 4
currently-registered affected scripts (baseline_1b_train, baseline_1b_train_scratch,
baseline_500m_train, train) via submit_sweep.py itself (launch.submit=false, piped 'yes' past the
AUTO-RESUME confirmations for the 3 already-completed baseline runs) and confirmed each now
references the local copy. baseline_1b_train_smoketest.sbatch/baseline_500m_train_smoketest.sbatch
are orphaned (no corresponding conf/runs/*.yaml entry any more, so submit_sweep.py can't regenerate
them) -- left hand-patched via sed, not proven correct the way the other 4 are.

Moved cosmos_dit_wm/ -> draft_code/cosmos_dit_wm/ and cosmos_dit_wm_experiments.py ->
draft_code/cosmos_dit_wm_experiments.py. Confirmed via repo-wide grep afterward: zero remaining
references to the old path outside draft_code/ (besides this journal's own historical narration).
draft_code/ itself is confirmed NOT auto-imported by anything (no __init__.py, not in
pyproject.toml's packaged paths, outside the one swept config.experiment dotted path) -- it's
genuinely inert until something explicitly imports from it again.

--- New feature: task_names (train) / task_keyword (eval) -- select a subset of a suite's tasks
without copying/symlinking data ---

Motivated by wanting a fast single-task confirmation run (see below) without repeating the
copy-a-directory approach first used for this (a throwaway .smoketest_data_ketchup/ dir, since
deleted) -- user explicitly asked not to keep copying data around.

Root cause: LIBERODataset (datasets/libero_dataset.py) had no task-filtering knob at all; its only
input is `data_dir`, and get_hdf5_files(data_dir) (dataset_utils.py) walks it for every .hdf5 file
found, unconditionally. Added:
  - filter_hdf5_files_by_task_names(hdf5_files, task_names) in dataset_utils.py -- case-insensitive
    substring match against each file's basename; raises (lists available files) if task_names is
    given but nothing matches, rather than silently loading everything.
  - LIBERODataset.__init__ gained an optional `task_names` param, applied to both hdf5_files and
    rollout_hdf5_files right after get_hdf5_files(). Deliberately does NOT change how
    dataset_statistics.json is resolved (still looked up by data_dir, not derived from the filtered
    file list) -- so pointing data_dir at the real suite dir as usual and filtering via task_names
    keeps the full-suite normalization stats, no separate copied directory needed at all.
  - RunConfig.task_names (sweep.py) + submit_sweep.py's train-type branch: threads into
    `dataloader_train.dataset.task_names=[...]` automatically when set.
  - Extended to the 3 KD-dataset build scripts too (build_distill_dataset.py,
    build_synthetic_distill_dataset.py, build_teacher_native_distill_dataset.py): added
    `task_names: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)` to each of their 3 params.py dataclasses
    (BuildDistillDatasetParams/BuildSyntheticDistillDatasetParams/
    BuildTeacherNativeDistillDatasetParams), a `--task_names` argparse flag (nargs="+") to each
    script, and threaded RunConfig.task_names into all 3 of submit_sweep.py's build-branch generated
    commands. This means redoing the full 6-variant KD sweep on 1-task data (an earlier request this
    session, not yet executed) no longer needs any data copying either -- just
    `suites=[libero_object_regen] task_names=[<name>]` on all 3 build runs.
  - Verified end-to-end via dry runs (no GPU needed): build_distill_dataset_std_0_05 with
    suites=[libero_object_regen] task_names=[ketchup] correctly generates
    `--data_dir .../libero_object_regen --task_names ketchup` (real suite dir, no copy); same for
    the baseline_500m_train training path. Unit-tested filter_hdf5_files_by_task_names and the
    OmegaConf List[str]-with-default-factory pattern directly (both clean); full-module import
    blocked by the usual login-node CUDA/activate_cuda_env.sh gap (libcublas.so.13), unrelated to
    these changes -- py_compile clean on every edited file.

Eval-side had the identical gap, worse: periodic_libero_eval_static.py's own docstring claimed
"every task... this is the only knob" even though run_libero_eval.py already supports --task_ids --
this script just never passed it through. Fixed by reusing periodic_libero_eval.py's existing
resolve_task_id(task_suite_name, task_keyword) helper (that script's own module docstring already
flagged --task_id/--task_ids as a mismatched-flag bug worth NOT repeating here): added
--task_keyword to periodic_libero_eval_static.py's CLI (resolves to a numeric --task_ids at
startup, logged), threaded through run_eval()'s subprocess command; added RunConfig.eval_task_keyword
+ RunConfig.eval_every_n_iters (the underlying script already supported --eval_every_n_iters, just
never exposed via submit_sweep.py) and threaded both into the kd_static_eval branch. Confirmed
resolve_task_id('libero_object', 'ketchup') -> (4, 'pick up the ketchup and place it in the basket')
directly (no CUDA needed, pure libero-benchmark lookup) and dry-ran the full generated eval command.

--- Task selection for the single-task run: re-examined per-task evidence, corrected an
overclaim ---

Wanted a task baseline_500m_train reliably masters early, to use as a fast (~3hr) confirmation run
instead of a full 30k-iteration/10-task sweep. Re-checked this run's own per-task breakdowns already
in this journal (iter 1400 sub-entry) rather than guessing: ketchup was perfect (3/3) at iter 1400,
one of only 2 tasks perfect that early. Initially proposed max_iter=3000 on that basis.

When asked directly "does it reliably master that task by 2000", checked more carefully instead of
assuming yes: the run's own AGGREGATE success rate collapsed to 0.0% at iters 1600 AND 1800 (no task
passing even one trial), and only 3.3% (1/30, i.e. at most one task/one trial) at iter 2000 -- so
ketchup was almost certainly failing right around iter 2000, a real regression from its iter-1400
perfect score, not noise on some other task. The iter-2200 per-task breakdown lists "salad dressing,
tomato sauce" as the 2 perfect tasks -- ketchup is NOT among them, consistent with still not
recovered. It reappears perfect at iter 2600 (one of 6 tasks). Corrected conclusion: ketchup's
trajectory is perfect -> regress -> recover, not monotonically mastered from 1400 onward; "reliably
mastered by 2000" is not supported, but "mastered again by 2600, with margin inside a 3000-iteration
budget" is. Chose max_iter=3000 on that corrected basis. Flagged to the user that this is still only
2 isolated per-task breakdowns (1400, 2600) plus indirect aggregate evidence for 1600-2200, not a
continuous per-checkpoint curve for ketchup specifically -- offered (not yet done) to pull a real
continuous single-task curve from the raw per-checkpoint eval logs before trusting this further.

Also separately confirmed (per user question): NONE of the kd_static_500m_std_0_05* variants (bare,
_av, the 6 _syn_tn/_syn_tn_pa ones) ever exceeded 50% success at any point -- ceiling across all of
them is 3.3% (syn_tn's final iter-59999 score); kd_static_500m_std_0_05_av's eval crashed on every
single checkpoint (status=failed throughout, not even a real 0%); kd_static_500m_std_0_05_action has
no eval data at all. Only the non-KD baseline_500m_train/baseline_1b_train runs reach 50%+.

--- Provenance check on regenerate_libero_dataset.py + a real recipe-mismatch finding vs. the
Cosmos Policy paper ---

Side investigation triggered by "is ketchup's 42-demo count really 10 trajectories?" (it isn't --
LIBERO's full release is 50 demos/task; ketchup's regenerated file has 42, i.e. 8 of the original 50
failed to replay during regeneration and were dropped -- see next paragraph). Confirmed
regenerate_libero_dataset.py (experiments/robot/libero/) is vendored, unmodified upstream Cosmos
Policy code: NVIDIA copyright header, and `git log --follow` shows exactly ONE commit ever touching
it -- a32e01f "add base cosmos policy code and setup and demo code" (2026-07-20), the initial bulk
import. Never edited since.

Checked its 3-line docstring ("we save at 256x256 instead of 128x128" / "filter no-op actions" /
"filter unsuccessful demonstrations") against the actual Cosmos Policy paper
(arxiv.org/abs/2601.16163, Section 5.1 -- confirmed this is the right paper: abstract's 98.5%
LIBERO / 67.1% RoboCasa success rates match this journal's own earlier "98.5% success rate ***"
teacher-checkpoint reference). Fetched via WebFetch (abs page had no implementation detail; PDF
exceeded WebFetch's 10MB fetch limit; the HTML rendering at arxiv.org/html/2601.16163 worked).

Results: the paper confirms "50 demonstrations each" per task (500 total across libero_object's 10
tasks) and does say demos get filtered for lack of success -- but with a caveat this repo's
docstring doesn't carry: "Following Kim et al. (2024), we filter unsuccessful demonstrations for
policy training but use the full unfiltered set for world model and value function training." The
256x256-vs-128x128 resolution note and the no-op-action filter aren't mentioned in the paper at all
(silent, not contradicted -- plausibly just below the paper's level of detail).

Why this matters beyond a docstring nitpick: every dataset this KD sweep is actually built from
(kd_distill_dataset_std_0_05, and both synthetic builds) sources from
LIBERO-Cosmos-Policy/success_only/<suite>_regen -- the directory name says it outright, this is the
success-filtered set, applied uniformly with no policy-vs-world-model-vs-value carve-out anywhere in
this repo's data pipeline. Per the paper's own stated recipe, that's the right set for the
action/policy loss term but arguably the WRONG one for the future-state/value loss terms that
several of the 6 sweep variants actually train (av_syn_tn/syn_tn and their _pa siblings all have a
value and/or future-state objective, not just action). Not yet confirmed whether this was a
deliberate simplification when the KD pipeline was originally built or an unnoticed gap -- no
unfiltered ("all_episodes"-style, pre-success-filtering demo) dataset variant currently exists
anywhere in /project storage to compare against; would need a fresh regenerate_libero_dataset.py
run without the success filter (or a code change to stop filtering) to actually test whether this
makes a measurable difference to the value/future-state heads specifically. Flagged, not yet
investigated further or acted on.

--- Eval companion (20065873) never actually started -- blocked by a week-long, all-node
maintenance reservation, not ordinary contention; cancelled and resubmitted with a shorter
walltime ---

20065871 (training) started running immediately, but 20065873 (eval) sat PENDING the whole time
with reason "ReqNodeNotAvail, Reserved for maintenance" -- not the ordinary Priority/resource wait
20065871 got. `scontrol show reservation` explains why: ReservationName=MAINT20260902,
StartTime=2026-09-02T09:00:00, EndTime=2026-09-09T09:00:00 (a full 7-day maintenance window),
Flags=MAINT,IGNORE_JOBS,SPEC_NODES,ALL_NODES, covering ~780 nodes -- effectively the entire
cluster. baseline_500m_train_eval.yaml's default --time=23:59:00, requested from ~15:38 on
2026-09-01, would still be running past 09:00 the next morning -- exactly when this reservation
locks the whole cluster down for a week -- so Slurm's scheduler refuses to start it at all, not
just delays it: with the reservation lasting until 2026-09-09, waiting would have meant the eval
job effectively never ran this session. 20065871's own 4hr walltime request comfortably finishes
tonight, well clear of the cutoff, which is why only the eval job was affected.

Fix: `scancel 20065873`, then resubmitted the identical config with time_limit lowered to
14:00:00 (comfortable margin over the ~17.5hr gap remaining before the reservation start, and well
over training's own ~3hr expected runtime) --

  job_id 20069105  cosmos_kd_baseline_500m_train_1task_ketchup_eval
    same overrides as 20065873 (monitored_job_name=cosmos_kd_baseline_500m_train_1task_ketchup,
    eval_task_keyword=ketchup, eval_num_trials_per_task=5, eval_every_n_iters=100), only
    time_limit changed (23:59:00 -> 14:00:00).

Confirmed via squeue immediately after resubmit: PD, reason "(None)" -- ordinary queue wait now,
no longer blocked by the reservation.

Also switched loss-monitoring approach for 20065871 mid-run: the first background Monitor polled
every 30s and reported every single new train_loss.csv row -- correctly working, but far too
noisy for a ~3hr run (~350+ notifications). Stopped it and restarted polling every 5 minutes,
reporting only a progress digest every 250 iterations, any NaN loss, real stderr error signatures,
or the job's final terminal state -- ~12 notifications total instead of hundreds, same crash-
detection coverage.

--- Prep done this session (all on Vulcan) ---

T5 text embeddings regenerated -> LIBERO-Cosmos-Policy/success_only/t5_embeddings.pkl (what every
conf/runs/*.yaml's t5_text_embeddings_path points at). Two snags worth recording:
  - First attempt died with a cryptic `TypeError: not a string` from SentencePiece. Cause: a
    partial HF download of google-t5/t5-11b -- config.json came down but spiece.model /
    tokenizer.json did not, so transformers silently fell back to the slow tokenizer with
    vocab_file=None. Re-running the fetch (login node, has internet) completed the files and fixed
    it. Now documented in KD_README.md step 1.
  - OOM-killed at --mem=32G while loading the 45 GB fp32 t5-11b checkpoint into host RAM (T5Encoder
    Model.from_pretrained reads the whole state dict before discarding the decoder half). Succeeded
    at --mem=90G on 1x L40S; ~3:40 for the encoder load, <1s per embedding, 10 unique instructions.
  - NOTE: t5-11b is currently cached under $COSMOS_POLICY_STORAGE/huggingface/hub, NOT the
    $COSMOS_POLICY_STORAGE/hf_cache that activate_cuda_env.sh pins HF_HOME to (with HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1).
    Not yet moved. Harmless for the baselines (they read the precomputed .pkl, not the live
    encoder) but will matter for any teacher-loading / KD-dataset-build run.

Code change to make submit_sweep.py cluster-portable: it hardcoded `--gres=gpu:h100:*` in 11
places, which will never schedule on Vulcan (L40S only). Added RunConfig.gpu_type (sweep.py),
defaulting from $COSMOS_POLICY_GPU_TYPE (else "l40s"), and collapsed all 11 sites to
f"gpu:{run.gpu_type}:{run.gpus}". Also made the torchrun path pass --nproc_per_node={run.gpus}
(was hardcoded 1, ignoring run.gpus entirely). activate_cuda_env.sh now exports
COSMOS_POLICY_GPU_TYPE=l40s next to COSMOS_POLICY_STORAGE/ACCOUNT. Verified: default -> gpu:l40s:1;
`runs.<name>.gpu_type=h100 runs.<name>.gpus=2` -> gpu:h100:2 + --nproc_per_node=2; all 20 run
types still generate valid sbatch.

Prereqs that were already on Vulcan /project storage from the earlier migration and did NOT need
regenerating: LIBERO-Cosmos-Policy/success_only/libero_object_regen/ (+ its dataset_statistics*.json),
kd_inits/student_init_500m_dcp.

Both runs printed `[WARNING] ... already exists and is non-empty (no checkpoint yet, but other
files present ...)`. Benign: the <run_dir>/slurm/ dirs had been pre-created by dry-run
submit_sweep invocations earlier this session (submit_sweep mkdir -p's run_dir/slurm even on a dry
run), NOT by a real prior run. No checkpoint present -> training would have started from iter 0 off
the DCP init.

--- Result: training job 762566 FAILED after 27 s ---

exitcode 1, at config-import time, before any training step:

  run_train.py -> runpy scripts.train -> load_config -> _load_py_config -> config.config.py
    -> config.config_v2 -> ...defaults.ema -> models.text2world_model
    -> _src/imaginaire/utils/optim_instantiate.py -> _src/imaginaire/utils/fused_adam.py:17
       `import transformer_engine as te`
  ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'transformer_engine'

The Vulcan .venv has torch 2.9.1+cu128 and megatron_core 0.14.0 but no transformer_engine at all.
pyproject.toml / uv.lock only pin transformer-engine under the cu128 extra (==2.2.0, built for
torch 2.7) and the cu130 extra (==2.8.0, torch 2.9), both from the nvidia-cosmos dependency index;
neither is installed here, and the venv's torch 2.9+cu128 matches neither locked TE wheel's
(torch, cuda) pair. So `uv sync` as run on Vulcan has not produced a working training environment
-- consistent with commit ae1650e ("kd folder replication requirements ... this does not work well
yet").

Eval job 762567 was still RUNNING after this (separate entrypoint, hadn't hit the same import yet
or doesn't). It polls cosmos_kd_baseline_500m_train_v2/checkpoints/ which will now never be
written, so it will idle its full ~24 h walltime for nothing -- should be scancel'd. Whether the
eval / dataset-build entrypoints hit the same transformer_engine wall is untested.

--- Status / what's actually blocking ---

The Vulcan baseline-parity test is blocked on the TRAINING ENVIRONMENT, not on anything this
session was chasing (config paths, /project storage layout, GPU type -- all now sorted). Need a
transformer_engine install that matches torch 2.9+cu128 before any torchrun/Trainer entrypoint
runs on Vulcan. The gpu_type change, the T5 embeddings, and the _v2 naming scheme are all reusable
once that's fixed. No parity data yet -- nothing trained.

============================================================
2026-09-02 -- Vulcan training env FIXED (transformer_engine) + 1-iter smoketest passes;
base-model checkpoint prefetch + wandb-off were the two follow-on walls
============================================================

Picking up the blocker from the previous entry. Worked it interactively on one L40S
(salloc --account=aip-courvill --gres=gpu:l40s:1 --cpus-per-task=8 --mem=64G --time=3:00:00).

--- Fix 1: transformer_engine (the actual blocker) ---

Root cause was just that the Vulcan .venv had been built with a bare `uv sync` -- no CUDA extra,
so torch resolved to an unpinned 2.9.1+cu128 and transformer-engine (extras-only) was never
installed. Fix = build with the extra the setup script already intends:

  uv sync --extra cu130 --group libero --python 3.10

-> torch 2.9.0+cu130, transformer_engine 2.8.0, flash-attn, all the pinned nvidia-cu13 libs.
`import torch, transformer_engine, megatron.core` clean afterwards. (cu130, not cu128: Vulcan's
L40S driver is 13.2, and setup_cosmos_policy_uv.sh's own CUDA_EXTRA is already "cu130". cu128 is
the rrg-gberseth/torch-2.7 combo.)

Side note: `source activate_cuda_env.sh` after the re-sync spat ~40 `ln: File exists` errors from
.cuda_so_shims/ -- its `[[ -e link ]] || ln -s` guard is false for a *dangling* symlink, so every
shim left pointing at a now-removed cu128 .so retries and fails. Harmless (LD_LIBRARY_PATH still
set) but `rm -rf .cuda_so_shims` before sourcing clears it. Flagged in KD_README step 0b; not yet
fixed in the script itself (should just `rm -rf` the dir at the top of that block).

--- Fix 2: base-model checkpoints resolved at config-import time ---

Next wall, immediately after: config load ->
cosmos_policy_experiment_configs.py calls get_checkpoint_path("hf://...") at MODULE LEVEL (every
experiment registered eagerly on import), so run_train downloads these the moment the config
loads. activate_cuda_env.sh sets HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 + compute nodes are offline -> LocalEntryNotFound
/ OfflineModeIsEnabled from checkpoint_db.get_checkpoint_by_hf. The two eager URIs (+ one lazy
tokenizer) are:
  hf://nvidia/Cosmos-Predict2-2B-Video2World/model-480p-16fps.pt      ~3.9 GB   GATED
  hf://nvidia/Cosmos-Predict2-2B-Video2World/tokenizer/tokenizer.pth  ~0.5 GB   GATED (lazy)
  hf://nvidia/Cosmos-Policy-ALOHA-Predict2-2B/...pt                    ~3.9 GB   ungated

Only Cosmos-Predict2-2B-Video2World is gated (gated=auto -> instant on clicking "Agree and access
repository"). Needed `hf auth login` (token now at $COSMOS_POLICY_STORAGE/hf_cache/token, user
esraasaleh) then prefetched all three from the login node into $COSMOS_POLICY_STORAGE/hf_cache
(8.8 GB total; the setup script does exactly this set + the LIBERO ckpt/dataset). The KD *teacher*
(nvidia/Cosmos-Policy-LIBERO-Predict2-2B) is a separate ungated fetch, not needed for baselines.

NOTE: this is the FIRST time hf_cache/ has been populated on Vulcan -- it was empty. The t5-11b
encoder from the previous entry went to $COSMOS_POLICY_STORAGE/huggingface/ by mistake (not the
hf_cache/ that activate_cuda_env.sh pins HF_HOME to); harmless for now (baselines read the .pkl,
not the live encoder) but it should be moved.

--- Fix 3: wandb still prompted for an API key ---

submit_sweep.py already exported WANDB_MODE=offline and passed job.wandb_mode=offline. Changed
both to `disabled`: (a) offline still writes a local wandb/ run dir and, more to the point, (b)
the interactive smoketest without the override hit `wandb: Paste an API key` because the config
default is wandb_mode="online" and wandb.init(mode=...) as an explicit arg BEATS the WANDB_MODE
env var -- so `export WANDB_MODE=...` alone never worked for the torchrun path; job.wandb_mode is
the one that matters. Now `disabled` in both places -> wandb is a complete no-op, no dir, no
network. (submit_sweep.py + folder README output-tree updated.)

--- Result: 1-iteration smoketest PASSES on Vulcan ---

  torchrun --nproc_per_node=1 -m ...run_train -- experiment=cosmos_kd_student_500m_libero
    job.name=smoke_1iter_500m job.wandb_mode=disabled trainer.max_iter=1 ...
    checkpoint.load_path=$COSMOS_POLICY_STORAGE/kd_inits/student_init_500m_dcp

-> "[SUCCESS] Done with training.", checkpoint iter_000000001 written (DCP model/optim/scheduler/
trainer shards + latest_checkpoint.txt). DCP init loaded, one step ran, checkpoint saved. Env is
good end-to-end.

--- 2026-09-02 ~00:15 -- "slowdown" investigated: it's ~1.5x (L40S vs H100), not a regression;
walltime bumped 12h -> 36h; jobs relaunched ---

762717 ran healthily (loss 8.0 -> ~0.6 by iter 401, tracking the old run's curve band-for-band:
old/new means 7.92/7.90, 7.44/7.35, 4.38/4.29). Steady 4.5 s/iter -> 30k would be ~37.5h, way over
the 12h walltime. Investigated the per-iter cost:

  * GPU is NOT the question -- `srun --jobid=762717 --overlap nvidia-smi` on rack14-13:
    util 100% / mem-util 100% / power 322-327W of 350W / P0 / throttle reasons 0x0 / 24GB of 46GB.
    Pegged and near the power cap, no throttling. The training proc's single CPU core at 94% is
    keeping the num_workers=0 loader fed (GPU never stalls). Compute + memory-bandwidth bound.
  * The "~6x slower" from the previous entry was a BAD COMPARISON -- that 0.72 s/iter figure is
    profile_node_throughput's, which measures the kd_static workload (combined_kd_loss, real +
    synthetic double denoise() forward, random-init skip_load_model). The baseline here is the
    plain torchrun/Trainer path with the full experiment config (EMA, FSDP, the real joint
    objective). Not comparable.
  * The right comparison: the OLD rrg-gberseth baseline_500m_train's OWN train_loss.csv timestamps
    -> median 3.0 s/iter (1s timestamp resolution, so coarse; p90 4.0 s). So L40S is ~1.5x the
    H100 here -- well under the ~3x peak-bf16-FLOPS gap, consistent with the job being
    bandwidth-bound (L40S 0.86 TB/s vs H100 3.35 TB/s) rather than FLOPS-bound.
  * The 49s hit at iter 5 both runs (old and new) is the `compile_tokenizer` callback firing at
    its `compile_after_iterations: 4` -- one-off torch.compile of the tokenizer/VAE, not the DiT
    (the DiT is not compiled in this config). Expected.
  * The old H100 run ALSO never did 30k in one 12h job: it ran 24h49m for 25.3k iters across
    resumes and stopped at 25324/30000. The 12h walltime was always too short for this config.

Decision: no speedup worth chasing for a parity test (FP8 on L40S or torch.compile-ing the DiT
could give 10-30% but both change numerics / break bf16 parity with the H100 run). Instead bumped
`time_limit` 12:00:00 -> 36:00:00 in baseline_500m_train.yaml AND baseline_500m_train_eval.yaml
(36h ~= the ~37.5h a full 30k needs; routes to Vulcan's 3-day GPU tier gpubase_bygpu_b4). Both
yamls now carry a comment explaining the number.

Relaunched (scancel 762717/762718, and finally 762567 the orphan):
  762882  cosmos_kd_baseline_500m_train_v2         -- AUTO-RESUMES from iter_000000400 (checkpoint
                                                      saved before the scancel; ~1 iter lost)
  762883  ..._train_eval_v2
Both PENDING at submit: 762882 reason "Nodes required ... DOWN, DRAINED or reserved for higher
priority partitions" (generic backfill-wait message for the 3-day tier, which had 0 fully-idle
nodes but ~110 partially-free `mix` nodes -- a 1-GPU/40G/8-CPU job should backfill). If it sits
pending too long, fall back to 12:00:00 + resume.

--- 2026-09-02 ~03:50 -- RESUME crawls at ~175 s/iter (GPU idle); root cause not pinned down;
switched strategy to fresh-run-in-one-job + dataloader fix ---

762882 (resumed from iter 400 on rack10-16) ran ~3h and got from iter 400 to ~462 -- steady
169-195 s/iter, vs 762717's 4.5 s/iter. `nvidia-smi` on the node: GPU util 0%, power 93W (idle),
our python proc pegged at ~190% CPU in R state, node load average 43 (shared node, other users'
jobs at 100% CPU each). So: not GPU-slow, it's stuck on CPU between steps -- serial data
loading/decode (num_workers=0) starved on a contended node, OR something resume-specific.
CONFOUNDED: the only fast run (762717) was BOTH fresh AND on a different, less-loaded node
(rack14-13, load 28). 764339 (rack12-10) was cancelled after 45s so it never gave a clean
iteration-timing number. Not chasing the exact cause further right now.

Also found while wiping: the eval run_dir had eval_failure_iter_000000200.log and
eval_failure_iter_000000400.log -- 762718/762883 were FAILING to evaluate the checkpoints, not
just idling. Separate issue (the eval/inference path on Vulcan), not yet looked at; doesn't block
the training-loss parity comparison.

Fix applied:
  * submit_sweep.py + sweep.py: new RunConfig fields for the torchrun path --
      num_workers (was hardcoded 0; now configurable, persistent_workers auto-follows)
      stage_data_to_tmpdir (cp -rL the resolved data_dir to $SLURM_TMPDIR at job start, point
        dataloader_train.dataset.data_dir at the copy; no-op fallback to /project on failure)

--- 2026-09-03 -- NEW EXPERIMENT: teacher_on_demos (baseline recipe, teacher targets) ---

Question: does training the SAME student, on the SAME demo states, through the SAME
torchrun/Trainer path and joint EDM objective as baseline_{500m,1b}_train, but with the four
joint denoising targets (action chunk + future image + future proprio + value) taken from the
TEACHER's own generation instead of the human demo, beat plain BC on demos? This is the
torchrun-path analogue of the kd_static teacher-native idea, but with ZERO change to the
training/loss code -- only the dataloader's targets differ. Deliberately NOT a kd_* run type.

Why it can't be a drop-in relabeled dataset dir: the future obs at timestep t is also timestep
t+16's CURRENT obs, so rewriting it in the HDF5 corrupts another sample's input. The teacher
targets have to be a per-(episode,timestep) sidecar consumed at LIBERODataset.__getitem__ time.

Design + as-built notes: kd/TEACHER_ON_DEMOS_PLAN.md (committed alongside this). Shape of it:

  * kd/build_teacher_on_demos_dataset.py -- new run_type "build_teacher_on_demos". Reads through
    libero_object_regen via LIBERODataset (same kwargs as libero_all_4_suites_dataset EXCEPT
    rollout_data_dir="", use_image_aug=False -- deterministic clean current-obs for the teacher to
    condition on), enumerates every demo timestep once, runs
    synthetic_generation.generate_teacher_native_targets (the SAME teacher query
    build_teacher_native_distill_dataset.py makes -- get_data_and_condition ->
    generate_samples_from_batch(num_steps=1) -> extract_action/value/proprio +
    get_future_images_from_generated_samples), and writes a sidecar HDF5 tree mirroring the suite:
    <task>_demo.teacher_on_demos.hdf5, one group per demo_key, datasets action_chunks (T,16,7) /
    future_proprio (T,9) / value (T,) / future_{image,wrist}_image_jpeg (T,). Teacher action/
    proprio stored in normalized model space (used verbatim). value stored as teacher_value*2-1
    (generate_teacher_native_targets returns [0,1]; base LIBERODataset value_function_return is
    [-1,1]). Future images stored at the teacher's native 224 (NOT the demo's 256).
    Resumable at source-file granularity (root attr complete=True -> skipped); --task_names shards
    it by task; --max_episodes / build_max_episodes caps it for a smoketest (a truncated file is
    written complete=False so a real run rebuilds it).

  * LIBERODataset (datasets/libero_dataset.py): new kwarg teacher_on_demos_dir="" (default = exact
    no-op, byte-identical to before). When set, the sample_type=="demo" branch of __getitem__
    overrides action_chunk / future_proprio / value_function_return from the sidecar and swaps the
    teacher's future primary/wrist frames into decompressed_*_images[future_frame_idx]; also
    resizes image_list to final_image_size (224) before the concat so the 224 teacher frames stack
    with the 256 demo frames (real frames get exactly the 256->224 they'd get in preprocess_image
    anyway; teacher frames pass through untouched -- no 224->256->224 blur round-trip). next_*
    fields stay real (base loss never consumes them). Edge case future_frame_idx==relative_step_idx
    (last step): keep the real frame as "future", still apply the non-image targets. Missing
    sidecar / missing group -> FileNotFoundError/KeyError naming the path (no silent fallback).

  * sweep.py RunConfig: teacher_on_demos_dir (torchrun), teacher_on_demos_build_dir +
    build_num_workers + build_max_episodes (build). submit_sweep.py: _build_teacher_on_demos_params
    helper, "build_teacher_on_demos" dispatch branch, added to TEACHER_LOADING_RUN_TYPES (HF-cache
    prefetch) and a resume-aware main() one-shot branch (existing sidecar tree -> [INFO] RESUMES,
    not an overwrite prompt); torchrun branch stages the sidecar dir to $SLURM_TMPDIR alongside
    the suite (it's a SIBLING of the suite dir -- cp -rL of the suite alone doesn't pick it up) and
    injects dataloader_train.dataset.teacher_on_demos_dir.

  * kd/params.py: BuildTeacherOnDemosParams + loader.
  * conf/config.yaml: 6 defaults lines.
  * conf/runs/datasets/build_teacher_on_demos{,_smoketest}.yaml
  * conf/runs/teacher_on_demos_{500m,1b}_train{,_eval}.yaml -- byte copies of the baseline_*
    yamls + job_name + teacher_on_demos_dir + master_port (12345/12346, EADDRINUSE avoidance) +
    filename. The _eval companions are byte copies of baseline_*_train_eval.yaml (closed-loop
    LIBERO sim, no teacher targets) with only job_name/monitored_job_name changed -> success-rate
    curves directly comparable to baseline_{500m,1b}_train.
  * kd/inspect_teacher_on_demos.py -- sidecar sanity-check utility.

On branch teacher-on-demos. NOT committed yet.

Verified:
  * Off-GPU (synthetic + real-data shapes): read path serves teacher targets; current-obs video
    frames bit-identical to the baseline path; teacher_on_demos_dir="" a byte no-op; missing
    sidecar raises; build enumeration covers every (episode,step) exactly once with num_workers>0;
    submit_sweep composes all 53 runs, and the teacher_on_demos_500m torchrun command diffs from
    baseline_500m_train's only in job.name / master_port / loss_csv path / the added
    teacher_on_demos_dir override.
  * On GPU -- 772502 (build_teacher_on_demos_smoketest, max_episodes=2, ketchup): ran end to end
    on real libero_object_regen + the real 2B teacher (292 timesteps, 2 episodes), COMPLETED.
    inspect_teacher_on_demos.py on the output:
      - action_chunks (T,16,7) in ~[-1,1]; future_proprio (T,9); value (T,) in [-1,1] and RISING
        from ~-0.25 early to ~1.0 at episode end -- the *2-1 remap direction is correct (an
        inverted sign would silently train on reversed returns).
      - future_image / future_wrist decode to 224x224x3 uint8 (matches the resolution design).
      - both predicted frames: UPRIGHT, natural RGB, plausible LIBERO scenes -- no vertical flip,
        no BGR swap needed. (regenerate_libero_dataset's np.flipud is input-side only; confirmed
        it does not need mirroring on the model's decoded output.)

a margin, not a tight bound: a task is ONE source HDF5, and build_teacher_on_demos_dataset.py
writes a file's sidecar only after ALL its episodes finish (_write_sidecar requires every group
filled), so a mid-task timeout writes nothing and the task restarts from zero. (Per-episode
incremental flush would remove that all-or-nothing risk and make any walltime safe -- noted as a
possible follow-up, not done.) For an un-sharded all-10-tasks run, override time_limit to ~12h.

--- 2026-09-03 ~10:20 -- build_teacher_on_demos_dataset.py: PER-EPISODE incremental writes ---

Was: all-or-nothing per source file -- _write_sidecar built the whole <task>_demo.teacher_on_
demos.hdf5 in one torch.save-style write only after every episode of that file was in RAM. For a
single-file task like ketchup that meant one write ~1h in; a preemption/timeout at minute 55 lost
everything (file-level resume is useless when a task IS one file).

Now: each episode's HDF5 group is appended to the sidecar the moment its every timestep is
generated (h5py "a" mode, group attr episode_complete=True + f.flush() done LAST), then its RAM
buffer is freed. A rerun opens the partial sidecar, _completed_episodes() reads back the
episode_complete groups, and only the missing episodes are regenerated (--force deletes the
sidecar first). A crash mid-group leaves that group without episode_complete -> redone (the
per-episode write is idempotent, `if dk in f: del f[dk]`). When all of a file's episodes are
present, _finalize_sidecar() sets root complete=True (=False if max_episodes truncated the file;
per-episode resume still keeps what it built). Also: teacher is no longer loaded at all if a
resume finds nothing left to generate.

Tested off-GPU (copied the 3 helpers, that module can't import without CUDA): write 2/5 episodes
-> "crash" -> resume regenerates only 3/4/5 -> finalize; partial-group (no episode_complete)
rewrite is idempotent; LIBERODataset serves all 176 timesteps from the incrementally-built
sidecar. Also memory: only in-flight episode buffers held now (~1-2), not all 42.

Relaunched: 773232  (task_names=[ketchup], 3h, batch 8, incremental writes + flushed progress)

773232 COMPLETED in 02:20:33 (a bit slower than the ~1h guess -- the per-__getitem__ full-episode
256px image re-read is the real bottleneck, num_workers=8 doesn't fully hide it). Wrote all 42
ketchup episode groups incrementally, then finalized complete=True. Sidecar:
.../libero_object_regen__teacher_on_demos/pick_up_the_ketchup_..._demo.teacher_on_demos.hdf5
(217 MB). inspect_teacher_on_demos.py: all 42 groups pass -- action_chunks (T,16,7) in ~[-1,1],
future_proprio (T,9), value (T,) low early (~-0.35) rising to 1.0 at episode end (correct sign),
future_image/wrist decode to 224x224x3 uint8.

Then: build the other 9 task sidecars (submit_sweep build_teacher_on_demos task_names=[<t>], ~2.5h
each now, resumable per-episode; run several in parallel), commit the branch, launch the real runs.

--- 2026-09-03 ~10:15 -- 773572 in progress: loss matches baseline, closed-loop success = 0 ---

773572 (teacher targets) EDM loss tracks cosmos_kd_baseline_500m_train_1task_ketchup band-for-band
(0.20 vs ~0.22 at matched iters). But 773573's sim eval is 0/3 at every checkpoint 200..1800,
while the demo baseline was intermittently 1.0 from iter 1300 and consistently passing from ~1800.
So: latent-space denoising loss on the teacher targets is as low as the baseline's on demo
targets, yet the policy doesn't grasp.

Diagnosed by comparing the teacher action chunks (from the 773232 sidecar) against the demo's
recorded action chunks for the SAME 6341 ketchup states (normalized to [-1,1] via the suite
dataset_statistics.json):
  * translation (x,y,z): well matched -- |abs| ratio ~1.0, corr 0.78-0.98
  * gripper: identical -- 100% committed (|g|>0.5) in both, corr 0.97
  * within-chunk step-to-step |delta|: IDENTICAL (0.0229 both) -- teacher chunk is NOT smoother/
    smeared (that hypothesis was wrong)
  * ROTATION is heavily damped:
      yaw   : demo +0.25 +-0.13  ->  teacher -0.04 +-0.08   (|abs| 0.26 -> 0.055, ~5x attenuated,
                                                             mean sign-flipped, corr still +0.81)
      pitch : demo -0.08         ->  teacher +0.04           (halved, sign-flipped)
      roll  : std 0.16 -> 0.07   (halved)
  * RMSE(teacher - demo chunk) = 0.185 = 38% of the demo chunk RMS, concentrated in rotation.

Interpretation: extract_action_chunk_from_latent_sequence AVERAGES ~112 reverse-tiled copies of a
num_denoising_steps=1 (conditional-mean) teacher sample. Rotation is the low-energy part of the
action; both the averaging and the 1-step mean estimate shrink it toward zero far more than the
high-energy translation/gripper channels. The demo consistently applies +0.25 yaw to orient the
gripper for the ketchup grasp; the teacher target says ~0, so the student learns to under-rotate
-> reaches the bottle, closes the gripper, wrong grasp geometry -> 0% success. Loss is fine
because the loss is over the whole 9-frame latent (dominated by image reconstruction) and a
0.05-scale error on 3 of 7 action dims is a tiny fraction of it.

Possible fixes if confirmed after the run finishes: raise num_denoising_steps for the build
(a few real sampling steps -> sharper, less mean-reverted action); or take the teacher action
from a dedicated action-only extraction rather than the tiled-average; or per-dim rescale the
teacher action toward the demo action statistics; or accept that "teacher targets" here means
"teacher's cautious mean policy" and only compare on multi-task where BC-on-demos also isn't
saturated.

2026-09-03  teacher_on_demos ketchup -- rebuild sidecar at num_denoising_steps=5 (match eval)
================================================================================

Result of the first ketchup run (train 773572 COMPLETED @ iter 3000, loss 0.159 == demo
baseline; eval 773573 TIMEOUT after scoring iter 200..2800, ALL 0/3 -> 0% closed-loop).
Loss matched the demo baseline exactly, closed loop never completed a single episode.

Root cause confirmed: the sidecar was generated with num_denoising_steps=1, but real LIBERO
eval (run_libero_eval.py, which the kd_static_eval companion runs with no overrides) uses:
    num_denoising_steps_action = 5
    ar_future_prediction = False,  ar_value_prediction = False
  -> generate_future_state_and_value_in_parallel = True
  -> get_action() does ONE generate_samples_from_batch(num_steps=5) and reads action + future
     proprio + value + future images out of that single 5-step latent.
  (num_denoising_steps_future_state=1 / _value=1 in that config apply ONLY when ar_* is True --
   the config comments say "otherwise equal to num_denoising_steps_action".)
generate_teacher_native_targets already uses that exact one-joint-call structure
(num_conditional_frames = min_num_conditional_frames, all four outputs from one generated_sample),
so the ONLY mismatch was num_steps: 1 (build) vs 5 (eval). A 1-step sample is the teacher's
conditional mean; the reverse-tile-average action extraction then means it again -> the yaw
collapse in the action-error analysis above.

Changes (branch teacher-on-demos):

                                                             time_limit 3:00:00 -> 7:59:00
  * conf/runs/datasets/build_teacher_on_demos_smoketest.yaml num_denoising_steps 1 -> 5;

  * kd/params.py  BuildTeacherOnDemosParams.num_denoising_steps default 1 -> 5 (+ comments)

2026-09-03  785855  extraction diagnostic -- the tiled-AVERAGE is NOT the cause
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
kd/diagnose_action_extraction.py on 48 ketchup states (num_denoising_steps=5): pulled the raw
(16,28,28) action latent frame out of generated_sample, reshaped to the 112 reverse-tiled
(16,7) copies WITHOUT averaging, compared mean-over-copies vs copy[0] vs median vs the demo
action. Pooled over 768 chunk steps, normalized [-1,1]:

  dim               x        y        z     roll    pitch      yaw     grip
  demo |.|      0.167    0.401    0.336    0.116    0.148    0.257    1.000
  gen  |.|      0.176    0.357    0.348    0.141    0.086    0.056    1.000
  copy0|.|      0.175    0.357    0.348    0.141    0.087    0.059    1.000   <- == gen
  median|.|     0.176    0.357    0.348    0.141    0.086    0.056    1.000   <- == gen
  copy-std      0.014    0.013    0.014    0.012    0.011    0.013    0.015   <- UNIFORM across dims
  corr demo,gen  0.80     0.98     0.96     0.75     0.80     0.80     0.98
  corr demo,cp0  0.80     0.98     0.96     0.75     0.80     0.79     0.98   <- == gen

Conclusions:
  * H1 (reverse-tile-AVERAGE collapses rotation) -- REJECTED. copy[0] (no averaging) is
    identical to the mean on every metric; cross-copy spread is a uniform ~0.013 on ALL 7 dims
    (just residual sampling noise, same absolute size everywhere -- the teacher writes the same
    112-vector 112x). extract_action_chunk_from_latent_sequence is harmless.
  * H2 (teacher genuinely generates attenuated rotation) -- CONFIRMED. The teacher's own
    generated action at these states has:
      - translation x/y/z, gripper: faithful (|.| ratio ~1.0, corr 0.96-0.98)
      - yaw: |.| 0.056 vs demo 0.257  -> ~4.6x attenuated, mean sign-flipped (+0.24 -> -0.04),
        BUT still directionally correlated with the demo (corr +0.80)
      - pitch: halved + sign-flipped;  roll: ~same corr, slightly amplified
    So the teacher's rotation output tracks the demo's DIRECTION but is heavily scaled down --
    looks like L2 regression-to-the-mean on a low-variance dim, not multimodal cancellation
    (which would kill the correlation) and not an extraction artifact.

This is a genuine property of the frozen 2B teacher's single-query generation. Training on it
faithfully teaches the student attenuated yaw -> wrong grasp geometry -> 0% ketchup.

Open question, decisive: does the 2B teacher's OWN closed-loop eval complete ketchup with this
action profile? (run_libero_eval.py, teacher ckpt.) If yes -> the attenuation is compensated
somewhere in the real closed loop our single-query sidecar doesn't capture. If no -> "teacher
on demos" can't beat BC on a task the teacher itself can't do single-shot.
Alternative probe: per-dim rescale the sidecar's rotation toward demo stats, retrain, see if
success recovers -- directly tests "attenuated rotation is the killer".

npz: /home/esraa1/tod_action_diag/action_extraction_diag.npz
(Trailing OSError .nfsXXXX in the job log = multiprocessing NFS teardown, post-run, exit 0.)

2026-09-03  FINDING: dataset_statistics.json mismatch (local vs teacher) -- rotation only
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
While setting up the teacher self-eval I compared the two action-normalization stats files:

  libero_object_regen/dataset_statistics.json           (LOCAL -- what our LIBERODataset uses
                                                          for demo actions AND what run_libero_eval
                                                          unnormalizes student output with)
  nvidia/Cosmos-Policy-LIBERO-Predict2-2B/
      libero_dataset_statistics.json                     (TEACHER -- what the frozen 2B teacher
                                                          was trained + is evaluated with)

                x       y       z      roll    pitch    yaw     grip
  actions_min diff  +0.054  0.000   0.000  +0.108   +0.085  +0.035  0.000
  actions_max diff  0.000  -0.046   0.000  -0.179   -0.025  -0.194  0.000
  actions_std local 0.269   0.439   0.447   0.024    0.049   0.042   0.998
  actions_std teach 0.336   0.379   0.444   0.040    0.063   0.078   0.998

Translation (x,y,z) ranges and gripper are ~identical between the two files; ROTATION differs
a lot -- teacher's yaw range is 0.739 wide vs local 0.510 (1.45x), roll 0.614 vs 0.327.

Consequence for teacher_on_demos: the teacher GENERATES its action in the latent in ITS OWN
normalized space (the action latent frame was filled during teacher training via
replace_latent_with_action_chunk(teacher-normalized actions)). extract_action_chunk_from_latent_sequence
returns that verbatim, so the sidecar's `action_chunks` are TEACHER-normalized. But:
  - the student trains to reproduce them, then
  - run_libero_eval unnormalizes the student's output with the LOCAL stats
    (eval_dataset_stats_path = libero_object_regen/dataset_statistics.json).
=> systematic normalization mismatch, CONCENTRATED IN ROTATION (the only dims where the two
   files disagree). Exactly matches the symptom: translation+gripper fine, rotation broken, 0%.
The TEACHER_ON_DEMOS_PLAN.md assumption "teacher->raw->normalized is a lossy no-op" is WRONG --
it's only a no-op if the two stats files are equal, and they are not.

Caveat: a pure linear renormalization would preserve corr(demo,teacher); we measured yaw corr
0.80 (not ~1.0), so the mismatch explains the MAGNITUDE/scale error but there is ALSO a real
precision drop in the teacher's rotation. Both plausibly contribute to 0%.

Likely fix (option 2 from the prior entry, refined): in the build, convert the teacher's
generated action TEACHER-normalized -> raw (teacher stats) -> LOCAL-normalized (local stats)
before storing, so the sidecar matches the local convention the student trains+evals in.
Same for future_proprio (proprio stats also differ) and check value.

2026-09-04  BUG FOUND + FIXED: future_proprio renorm corrupted the target (regression)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
While double-checking eval normalization for the running 789317/789318 (still 0/3 through iter
2200, later than the demo baseline's own convergence at iter 1300-1800), re-examined the sidecar
future_proprio distribution -- the SAME teacher->local renorm applied to future_proprio as to
action turns out to badly corrupt it:

  future_proprio, post-renorm, all 9 dims pooled:
    dim4: min +0.99  max +5.89  mean +3.48   |.|>1 in 100% of states
    dim2: min -2.27  max +0.77  mean -1.22   |.|>1 in 64%
    dim6: min -0.34  max +1.99  mean +1.09   |.|>1 in 69%
    dim8: min -3.50  max +4.20  mean +0.93   |.|>1 in 49%
    overall: 32% of ALL future_proprio values exceed |1|, max magnitude 5.9
  (pre-renorm/teacher-norm: mean|.|=0.42, contained)

Root cause: action deltas are directly comparable across LIBERO suites (same control convention,
NVIDIA's 4-suite-pooled stats just widen the observed range) -- the raw round-trip is meaningful.
Proprio is an ABSOLUTE end-effector/joint position; different suites' tasks occupy different
regions of that space, so NVIDIA's pooled proprio range spans several disjoint workspaces:
  proprio_max dim4:  local 0.385  vs teacher 1.366  (3.6x wider)
  proprio_min dim2:  local -0.177 vs teacher -0.486
  proprio_max dim8:  local 0.103  vs teacher 0.636
Round-tripping future_proprio through raw units and re-normalizing with only libero_object's much
narrower slice of that pooled range throws it far outside [-1,1] on several dims. This is a
regression the "same fix as action" introduced, not a pre-existing problem -- and since
future_proprio is a training target in the SAME joint transformer as the action (not the executed
quantity, no reason it needed to match the local convention), squared-error terms of ~35 on that
frame (vs the normal ~0.1) plausibly explain why 789317 stayed at 0% past the point the baseline
converged.

FIX: kd/build_teacher_on_demos_dataset.py -- removed the future_proprio renorm call. action_chunks
still get teacher->local (that part is correct and verified, see the action-error artifact).
future_proprio now stored verbatim in the teacher's own normalization (as it always was pre-fix).
Root attrs: norm_convention="local_suite_actions__teacher_norm_future_proprio",
renorm_teacher_to_local="actions" (was "actions,future_proprio").

Actions taken:
  - scancel 789317, 789318 (training on the bad-proprio sidecar; not worth finishing)
  - archived (not overwritten): libero_object_regen__teacher_on_demos__5step_actionfix_badproprio/
      and the matching train/eval dirs __5step_actionfix_badproprio
  - fresh empty libero_object_regen__teacher_on_demos/ target dir
  - submitted 789704: build_teacher_on_demos task_names=[ketchup], num_denoising_steps=5,
    action-only renorm. params.yaml verified.
Next: when 789704 done, re-verify future_proprio is back in a contained range (and re-check
action stats are unchanged from the last good verification), then relaunch
teacher_on_demos_500m_train + _eval.

Rather than patch normalization field-by-field (action fixed, proprio reverted -- see prior

bug, not just an output-side one.

2026-09-04  790396 COMPLETED + verification + IMPORTANT re-analysis + relaunch
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Sidecar verification (teacher-norm throughout, no renorm):
  future_proprio: |.|>1 in 0.08% of values (was 32%), max abs 1.004 (was 5.9), mean|.|=0.401.
    FIXED -- contained, matches the pre-any-renorm baseline.
  action yaw: mean -0.038, |.|=0.064 -- same tiny magnitude as every un-renormalized version.

Re-analysis of the "rotation collapse": converted the DEMO's own commanded yaw (local-norm mean
+0.244) through raw units into the TEACHER's convention: raw = -0.3289 + (1.244/2)*0.510 =
-0.0117; teacher-norm = 2*(-0.0117+0.3643)/0.7393 - 1 = -0.046. The teacher's ACTUALLY GENERATED
yaw mean, -0.038, is almost exactly this (-0.046 vs -0.038) -- i.e. once compared in a consistent
unit system, the teacher's raw/physical yaw command roughly MATCHES what the demo itself commands.
The original "H2: teacher generates 5x-attenuated yaw" diagnosis (2026-09-03) compared teacher-norm
teacher output directly against local-norm demo reference WITHOUT unit conversion -- an
apples-to-oranges bug in that diagnostic script itself, not necessarily a real generation defect.
(The demo<->teacher correlation, 0.68-0.80 for rotation, IS unaffected by this -- correlation is
scale/shift-invariant, so that gap is genuine teacher imprecision, separate from the magnitude
question.)

Remaining real (separate) concern: EDM training adds noise in whatever NORMALIZED units the
target lives in (sigma up to ~85, sigma_data=0.5). A channel whose useful signal spans only
+-0.06 (teacher-norm yaw) sits far below the noise floor across most of the sampling schedule --
objectively harder for the STUDENT to recover precisely than the same physical command expressed
as a larger normalized number (e.g. local-norm's ~+-0.26), independent of whether the underlying
raw command is correct. This is a genuine SNR/training-dynamics argument for rescaling the
STUDENT's action TARGET to a larger-magnitude convention, orthogonal to the "is the raw physical
value right" question this section just resolved (mostly yes).

Decision: launch training on the current (teacher-norm-throughout, bug-free) sidecar AS-IS first,
as the clean read on whether this SNR/magnitude effect actually matters in practice. If still 0%
at iter 3000, that isolates the SNR/magnitude explanation (rules out both the conditioning bug and
the proprio corruption, since neither is present here) and justifies one more build: teacher-norm
conditioning + proprio (both correct, keep), but action rescaled to local (bigger training
signal) on top.

2026-09-04  K-sample (multi-sample, unaveraged) teacher_on_demos support added
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
User's choice: data-AUGMENTATION over averaging -- generate K independent teacher samples per
(episode, timestep) and store them SEPARATELY (not pre-averaged into one target), so the diffusion
student sees the teacher's genuine multimodality across training instead of a single noisy point
estimate (averaging first risks collapsing to an invalid target -- the mean of two valid-but-
different wrist orientations isn't necessarily itself a valid grasp -- and defeats the point of
training a diffusion policy to score-match a possibly-multimodal distribution).

Mechanism confirmed: generate_samples_from_batch's ONLY randomness is the initial diffusion noise
x_sigma_max = misc.arch_invariant_rand(shape, dtype, device, seed) -- np.random.RandomState(seed),
purely a function of `seed`, no reliance on global RNG state. Same data_batch (same conditioning)
+ different seed = different initial noise = a genuinely different sample from the teacher's
implied posterior. Calling generate_teacher_native_targets K times per batch with K distinct seeds
(seed_for_batch + k*1_000_003) gives K independent, reproducible draws.

Code changes:
  cosmos_policy/datasets/libero_dataset.py
    - _load_teacher_on_demos_sidecar: every field now (T,K,...) instead of (T,...); doc updated.
    - __getitem__: new `teacher_on_demos_k = random.randrange(K)` drawn once per call (same k
      across action_chunks/future_proprio/value/future_image*/future_wrist_image* -- one k is one
      coherent generation); all 5 indexing sites updated to [relative_step_idx, teacher_on_demos_k].
      Picked FRESH each __getitem__ call, so different epochs see different draws over training.
    - BREAKING change to the sidecar schema (no backward-compat shim) -- fine, no training run
      reads an old (T,...)-shaped sidecar going forward; all archived ones are historical only.
  kd/build_teacher_on_demos_dataset.py
    - _new_buffer(num_steps, num_teacher_samples): all arrays gain a K axis;
      future_image_jpeg/future_wrist_image_jpeg become list-of-K-lists.
    - _write_episode_group: datasets now (T,K,...); vlen image datasets (T,K); attrs +=
      num_teacher_samples.
    - build(): new `num_teacher_samples` param; main loop now `for k_idx in range(K):` calls
      generate_teacher_native_targets with seed_for_batch + k_idx*_SEED_STRIDE, collects per_k list
      (NOT averaged), scatters buf[field][rel, k_idx] = ... for every k; buf["filled"] still
      incremented once per state (after all K writes), so per-episode flush/resume logic unchanged.
    - module docstring: new "MULTIPLE TEACHER SAMPLES" section.
  kd/params.py: BuildTeacherOnDemosParams.num_teacher_samples: int = 1 (default = today's
    behavior, byte-identical sidecar shape otherwise -- just a size-1 K axis).
  sweep.py: RunConfig.num_teacher_samples: int = 1 (build_teacher_on_demos only).
  submit_sweep.py: _build_teacher_on_demos_params passes it through to params.yaml.

Data lineage (archived, all preserved):
  __1denoisestep/                1-step, teacher-norm both, K=1
  __5step_teachernorm/           5-step, teacher-norm both, K=1
  __5step_actionfix_badproprio/  5-step, action->local + BROKEN proprio->local renorm, K=1
  __teachernorm_k1/              5-step, teacher-norm throughout (correct conditioning), K=1
                                  (== the just-finished 791542/791543 run; train/eval dirs also
                                  archived as *__teachernorm_k1)
  libero_object_regen__teacher_on_demos/  5-step, teacher-norm throughout, K=5 (job 793246, current)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-04  Full-coverage K enumeration implemented (train-side, does not touch 793246)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Random-per-visit k selection (previous entry) under-utilizes K on a short run: with only ~3.8
epochs at max_iter=3000, E[distinct k's seen per state] = K*(1-(1-1/K)^3.8) ~= 2.9 of 5 -- roughly
2 of the 5 generated samples per state never get trained on at all. Implemented deterministic
full-coverage enumeration as an alternative mode, purely on the LIBERODataset (training) side --
does NOT touch the build script or the sidecar schema, so job 793246 (K=5 build) is completely
unaffected and kept running throughout.

cosmos_policy/datasets/libero_dataset.py:
  - new __init__ kwarg `teacher_on_demos_num_samples: int = 1`.
  - _calculate_epoch_structure: when teacher_on_demos_dir set and teacher_on_demos_num_samples>1,
    demo_base_count = self.num_steps * teacher_on_demos_num_samples (self.num_steps itself stays
    the raw per-state count) -> epoch_length = num_steps*K (calculate_epoch_structure's own
    zero-rollout branch is a straight passthrough, confirmed by reading dataset_common.py).
  - __getitem__ demo branch: teacher_on_demos_k_enum = idx // self.num_steps when in full-coverage
    mode (idx % self.num_steps still recovers the state as before) -- else None.
  - teacher_on_demos_k selection: teacher_on_demos_k_enum (deterministic) if set, else the
    existing random.randrange(K) fallback -- so K=1 (default) or omitting the new kwarg is a
    complete no-op, byte-identical to before.
  - assert teacher_on_demos_k_enum < K (the sidecar's ACTUAL per-episode K) so a
    teacher_on_demos_num_samples mismatch against the real sidecar fails loudly, not silently.

sweep.py: RunConfig.teacher_on_demos_num_samples: int = 1 (torchrun path).
submit_sweep.py: torchrun branch adds dataloader_train.dataset.teacher_on_demos_num_samples to
  overrides when != 1.

Consequence for the eventual training run: epoch_length becomes 6341*5 = 31,705 for ketchup (was
6341) -- steps/epoch at batch_size=8 goes from ~793 to ~3963. To get comparable per-state exposure
to the earlier K=1 run (~3.8 epochs), max_iter would need ~15,000; chose a lighter compromise for
the first K=5 run instead -- max_iter=6000 (~1.5 full passes over all 31,705 (state,k) pairs,
~3.8h wall time at the measured 2.3s/iter) -- adjustable once we see how it's converging.
Dry-run verified: teacher_on_demos_num_samples=5 correctly appears in the generated sbatch's
torchrun overrides.

Not yet submitted -- waiting on 793246 (K=5 sidecar build) to finish first.

Closed-loop success rate (3 trials/checkpoint, ketchup):
  200-1000:  0/3
  1200:1.0  1400:0  1600:0  1800:1.0  2000:0  2200:0  2400:1.0  2600:0  2800:1.0

This is the first teacher_on_demos ketchup run to sustain success. Progression across the 4
serious attempts:
  1-step teacher-norm K=1        -> 0% every checkpoint
  5-step teacher-norm K=1 (791542) -> intermittent, peak 100% @ 1600-1800, 0% at final ckpt
  5-step action->local + bad proprio renorm K=1 -> cancelled (proprio blow-up bug)
  5-step teacher-norm K=5 full-coverage (828271) -> holds ~100% from iter 3000-6000  <-- THIS

What did it: (a) teacher-norm-throughout normalization (correct teacher conditioning + no
per-field renorm, from the 791542 run) + (b) K=5 independent samples per state, stored unaveraged,
every one visited once per epoch (full-coverage). (b) is what converted 791542's "peaks then
collapses" into a stable policy -- consistent with the label-noise / diffusion-multimodality
argument: one teacher draw per state is too noisy a target; 5, seen as separate examples, let the
EDM loss learn the actual conditional distribution.

2026-09-08  Artifacts for the K=5 result + a chart-scale fix
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
- Metrics artifact for the K=5 run had a latent bug: the template's success/loss chart x-axis was
  hardcoded to a [0, 3000] domain (px = i => .../3000), so every checkpoint past iter 3000
  (i.e. 3200..6000) was drawn off the right edge and invisible -- the data was all present.
  Fixed: x-scale now computes MAXIT from the data and the ticks adapt.
  https://claude.ai/code/artifact/515e3a3d-cb33-4570-8004-733b9f57af35

- New artifact -- the training objective for the K=5 agent, math rendered (MathJax tex-svg):
  "K=5 Teacher Objective"  https://claude.ai/code/artifact/28fe1b72-4c6a-44d0-a180-54607ce3dfb7
  Summary of the objective (for the record):
    * Same joint EDM diffusion loss as the demo baseline. ONLY the clean latent x_0 changes.
    * The 4 denoising targets (action chunk / future image / future proprio / value) are 100%
      teacher-generated -- no ground-truth term, no blending (that mixing is the KD path's
      combined_kd_loss, not this torchrun path). The demo supplies the STATES and the current
      OBSERVATION the teacher is conditioned on; the teacher supplies the answers.
    * Per state s (6341 ketchup timesteps), draw K=5 independent teacher samples
      xi^(s,k) ~ q_phi(.|o(s),l) -- one reverse-diffusion pass each (num_steps=5, action slot
      free, num_conditional_frames=4), from independently seeded initial noise (arch_invariant_rand
      is seeded purely by the `seed` arg -> distinct seeds = distinct draws).
    * Build x_0^(s,k) = Phi( sigma_d * E(V_aug^(s,k)) ; a~, p_cur, p'~, v'~ ), everything in the
      TEACHER's own dataset_statistics normalization (student trains + evals in that same
      convention -- dataset_stats_override_path).
    * l_EDM(theta; x_0, c) = standard EDM x0-prediction loss, kappa=10,
      lambda(sigma)=(sigma^2+sigma_d^2)/(sigma*sigma_d)^2, hybrid schedule, unmasked over all
      N_lat = 9*16*28*28 = 112,896 latent elements.
    * Full objective: L(theta) = (1/NK) sum_s sum_k E_aug l_EDM(theta; x_0^(s,k), c(s)),
      every (s,k) a SEPARATE training example, epoch = N*K = 31,705, max_iter=6000 ~= 1.5 passes.
    * This is a K-sample Monte-Carlo estimate of the forward-KL projection
      min_theta E_s KL( q_phi(.|s) || p_theta(.|s) ) -- the student score-matches the teacher's
      WHOLE conditional, not one draw. That is the K=1 -> K=5 delta; nothing else in the loss
      changed.

2026-09-08  Experiment A: K=1 teacher-norm, RESUME 791542 from iter 3000 -> 6000
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Purpose: settle the K=5 confound. 791542 (K=1 teacher-norm) was max_iter=3000 and collapsed;
828271 (K=5) was max_iter=6000 and held. Two variables changed. This resumes 791542's exact
trajectory (iter_3000 checkpoint has full DCP train state: model/optim/scheduler/trainer) to
6000, so the ONLY difference from 828271 is K=1 vs K=5.
  - If it also holds ~100% at 3200-6000 -> "more iterations" was the fix, K=5 unnecessary,
    10-task build gets ~5x cheaper.
  - If still intermittent/collapsed -> K=5 is doing the work.

Code: cosmos_policy/datasets/libero_dataset.py -- added a backward-compat shim to
_load_teacher_on_demos_sidecar (reverses the earlier "BREAKING change, no shim" note): a pre-K
sidecar (action_chunks (T,chunk,7), no sample axis) gets a size-1 K axis inserted on load
(action_chunks[:,None] etc.), so __getitem__'s k-indexing resolves to 0 and everything downstream
is uniform. Needed because 791542's sidecar (__teachernorm_k1, built by job 790396) predates the
K-axis schema. teacher_on_demos_num_samples NOT passed -> default 1 -> epoch structure unchanged,
identical to what 791542 trained on.

2026-09-08  Experiment A RESULT: K=1 does NOT hold at the K=5 schedule -> K=5 is doing the work
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
844860 train COMPLETED 20:48:52 (5:02 elapsed incl. the pre-3000 replay), iter 6000, final loss
0.13. 844861 eval scored every checkpoint 3200-6000 by 20:51, then idled to the 23:45 walltime
cap (TIMEOUT, no data lost -- same idle-post-training pattern as the K=5 eval 828272).

Closed-loop success (K=1 teacher-norm, 3 trials/ckpt), resumed range:
  3200:0.33 3400:0   3600:0   3800:0.67 4000:0.67
  4200:1.0  4400:1.0 4600:1.0 4800:1.0  5000:1.0
  5200:0    5400:1.0 5600:0.67 5800:1.0 6000:1.0
  - 3200-3600: near-zero (resume transient, model re-stabilising off the iter-3000 checkpoint).
  - 4200-5000: five straight 1.0 -- briefly looked matched to K=5.
  - 5200: HARD collapse to 0/3, then 0.67 at 5600. Mean over 4200-6000 = 0.87.

K=5 (828271) over the same 3200-6000 window: 1.0 at every checkpoint except a single 0.667 at
5400 -- 15/15 >= 0.667, mean ~0.98, never a zero anywhere from iter 3000 on.

VERDICT: "more iterations" is NOT the fix. K=1 at the full 6000-iter schedule still carries the
intermittent full-collapse failure mode (0/3 at iter 5200, well past the resume transient) that
K=5 never exhibits. The full-coverage K=5 result's stability is attributable to K=5, not the
longer schedule. Consistent with the label-noise / diffusion-multimodality argument: one teacher
draw per state is too noisy a regression target regardless of how long you train on it.

Implication for the 10-task / all-suites head-to-head: K=5 is required. No ~5x shortcut -- the
per-task sidecars are the larger K=5 ones (~1.08 GB/task measured on ketchup, ~12 GB for all 10
libero_object tasks, ~45-50 GB for all 4 suites at K=5). One build job per task: 10 for
libero_object now (the only suite staged on Vulcan); 40 for all 4 standard suites once
spatial/goal/10 *_regen demos are brought over (currently broken symlinks under
LIBERO-Cosmos-Policy/success_only). Per-task K=5 build ~13.75h (job 793246) -- bump the build
yaml's time_limit above its 7:59 default or rely on per-episode resume + requeue.

Artifact updated (same URL, K=1 run's own metrics artifact, now 3000->6000 + confound verdict +
adaptive x-scale): https://claude.ai/code/artifact/0dee1752-384e-4e48-880d-b2b6e316a77d
